### L1.S16 · [T] · Rose (reflecting)

*Purpose: the suitors, named as dupes — what it costs to be defended by being pitied.*

> *(She thinks of them one by one, the way one touches a bruise to learn its shape. Laurent, reading it over coffee — amused at first, until the amusement thins: the foreigner's vulnerability, the man "forgiven for not knowing an English swindle."* Forgiven *— as if he needed forgiveness, as if his only crime were trusting an English girl with an English name. She wants, suddenly and fiercely, to write to him — not to explain, but to refuse the forgiveness on his behalf, the way one declines a tip one has not earned.)*
> *(Julian, reading it among men who have lent him money and will now lend him* looks *— going white around the mouth the way he does when really angry, the anger that has nowhere gentlemanly to go. She knows that whiteness. It is the color of a man deciding whether fists or lawyers are the appropriate response to ink, and finding both inadequate.)*
> *(Henry, at the Horse Guards, handed it with a grin and a "have you seen" — standing very straight, saying nothing. The straightness is the whole of it. Henry Beaumont does not defend with words; he defends by remaining upright in the vicinity of the insult, as if posture were an argument. It is, she thinks, not the worst argument available.)*
> *(And Octavia, setting it down and returning to her sitters: a penny paper is, to Octavia, an interruption — something between her and the north light, to be moved aside like a bad brush. Rose envies that. She envies it the way one envies a room with the door closed.)*
> *(They are called dupes. The word sits in her like a stone. It is not the insult to* her *that frightens her — she has been insulted by professionals. It is the insult to* them*: the suggestion that every man who has stood near her is either a knave or a fool. And underneath the anger, the fear she will not say aloud — that one of them might someday, in a tired hour, late, alone, wonder if the penny paper had a point. She touches the bruise. It holds its shape.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Permit the bruise-touching inventory — or refuse it as self-pity. → Remembered:Her discipline with feelings; it determines her confessions.
> 2. [social]Answer Laurent's "forgiveness" — refuse it for him, or let it stand. → Remembered:How she defends his dignity; the Comte notes the handling.
> 3. [social]Julian among the money-lenders — intervene, warn, or trust his anger. → Remembered:Her management of his pride; fury is asset or liability by her handling.
> 4. [stance]Henry "standing very straight, saying nothing" — ache, admiration, or a sparing letter. → Remembered:Her tenderness toward Henry; the romance register is set.
> 5. [examine]Octavia's uninterruptibility — envied, borrowed, or recognized. → Remembered:What she takes from Octavia's steadiness; her model for reserve.
> 6. [stance]Face the fear — one of them someday wondering if the paper had a point. → Remembered:Her honesty about it; it shadows every letter she writes.
> 7. [micro]Carry "dupes" like a stone — or set it down. → Remembered:What she keeps of the insult; carried stones shape posture.
> 8. [choice]Tell them she's thinking of them — or keep it private. → Remembered:Whether her care reaches them; suitors answer what they receive.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S17 · [D] · Rose, Augusta

*Purpose: "the restoration as swindle" — the third charge; Augusta on what can be proved.*

> *(They are walking in the garden; the morning's policy requires air. Augusta carries the secateurs, which means she intends to deadhead roses while delivering doctrine — the general's way of making counsel look like gardening.)*
> "The third charge is the cleverest." — Augusta "Fraud in the ruin. Dupes for suitors. But the restoration as *swindle* — that is the present tense, child. That is the charge that follows you into every ballroom, because every ballroom is where the restoration is being *financed*."
> *(The present tense. Rose feels it land — the first two charges are history, arguable, done. The third is* now*: every gown she wears, every dinner she gives, every nail driven at Hartwell Park will be read as evidence. She is financing a restoration in public, which means she is, in Pyke's arrangement, committing the swindle in public.)*
> "It is financed by — " Rose begins.
> "By your nerve, your grandmother's settlements, the Park's rents, and money borrowed honestly and repaid honestly. I know." — Augusta (snipping a dead rose-head with more force than the task requires) "*I* know. But Pyke has arranged it otherwise. The answer is not to argue accounts in public. The answer is to be *seen* until the arrangement collapses under the weight of the woman. You cannot *prove* a restoration honest, child. You can only *be* honest, repeatedly, in public, until the ton gets bored of suspecting you."
> *(Snip. Another dead head falls with more force than required. Rose watches her grandmother's hands and understands that the force is the tell — Augusta is worried, and Augusta's worry comes out as horticultural violence. It is almost comforting. Almost.)*
> "Win in the open." — Rose
> "Win in the open." — Augusta "The only court we can afford — and the only one whose verdicts *last*."*
> *(The only court we can afford. Rose does the arithmetic the solicitor would do: a libel action costs money they have not got, for a verdict the town would read as purchased. The ton's court costs only appearances — appearances she must make anyway. It is not justice. It is, she reflects, snipping a dead head herself with rather less force than Augusta, at least a court she can enter without borrowing the entry fee.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh "present tense" — why the third charge follows her; map its ambushes. → Remembered:Rose learns the libel's calendar, not just its text; she'll anticipate each week.
> 2. [stance]"You cannot prove a restoration honest" — impossibility, rage, or freedom. → Remembered:Her peace with unprovability; it governs whether she argues or appears.
> 3. [examine]Augusta deadheading "with more force than required" — the general's tell. → Remembered:She learns to read Augusta's hands; the tell returns when worry is hidden.
> 4. [choice]Itemize the true financing as her private brief. → Remembered:Nerve, settlements, rents, honest borrowing; Quill builds on this list.
> 5. [social]Who hears the financing itemized — Quill, Lavinia, or no one yet. → Remembered:How she rations money-truth; allies' counsel follows what they're told.
> 6. [examine]Test "the only court we can afford" — its charges, in what coin. → Remembered:Her understanding of the ton's price; it budgets every appearance.
> 7. [stance]Say "win in the open" back — echo, oath, or question. → Remembered:How she carries the refrain; her answer to every shortcut.
> 8. [micro]Snip a dead head herself — or leave it to Augusta. → Remembered:Whether she shares the general's rituals; small acts bind them.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S18 · [T] · London (observed)

*Purpose: the town has the paper too — the libel, traveling; the season's weather, changing.*

> *(By noon the* Lantern *is everywhere in London. Rose walks to the bookseller's for string and hears her own name twice before the street's end — once from a pair of shop girls, once from a cabman to his fare, both in* that tone*: the particular relish of the town handling a scandal the way one handles a hot dish, with noise and care. The difference is the whole of the gutter's power: it need not convince the town. It needs only to make the town* mention *her, in that tone, until the mentioning is the weather.)*
> *(A woman with a basket stops mid-sentence as Rose passes — the sentence dying in the air like a cut string. Rose nods. The woman nods back, recovers, finishes the sentence at a lower pitch. At the bookseller's, the string is measured and cut with excessive ceremony — service making up what he lacks in news, having already bowed lower, the particular bow of a tradesman who has read the paper and decided to stand by the customer. She buys the string. Two young ladies ask for novels and mean the* Lantern *— the bookseller's assistant fetches it without blinking, the way one fetches any other requested item.)*
> *(By the homeward turn, the mentioning has become weather: present, impersonal, endurable. She is no longer bracing for it — she walks the long way home practicing being mentioned, practicing the face, practicing the walk, and somewhere along the second street she realizes she has stopped bracing. That — the* unbracing *— frightens her more than the mentions did. Bracing was honest. What is this new ease? Adaptation, or surrender wearing adaptation's coat? The walk, practiced, carries her home regardless. Penny by penny, the bell rings on.)*

> *(The bell rings from two streets over — the boy with the papers, doing his rounds, crying the* Lantern *like any other ware. Penny by penny. She thinks of Pyke counting those pennies somewhere over his press, and finds she cannot hate him properly — he is a tradesman, after all, selling what the town wants to buy. The town wants her. Mentioned, discussed, revised. Very well: she will be mentioned, then, on her own terms — the walk, the face, the weather. She turns the key in the street door and goes in to luncheon, the string in her pocket, the practicing done for the day. Tomorrow: the same walk. The day after: the same. Weather, endured daily, becomes climate.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Study "that tone" — curiosity, malice, or appetite — in mentioning mouths. → Remembered:Her ear for the town's register; she'll hear shifts before she's told.
> 2. [stance]Practice the walk — as drill, performance, or prayer. → Remembered:How she frames the discipline; it sustains or hollows her.
> 3. [examine]The bookseller's "excessive ceremony" — apology, or verdict. → Remembered:Her read of small-town loyalty; it tells her who stands in London.
> 4. [micro]Finish the errand — buy the string — or forget it in the practicing. → Remembered:Whether small errands survive fire; the string ties to S1's talisman.
> 5. [stance]Meet the unbracing — frightened, welcoming, or interrogating. → Remembered:Her honesty about adaptation; it warns when ease becomes carelessness.
> 6. [social]The basket-woman, stopped mid-sentence — meet her eye, nod, or look away. → Remembered:How Rose meets the town's gaze; Bath remembers manners longer than libel.
> 7. [examine]Test "the mentioning has become weather" — its comfort, and its lie. → Remembered:Whether weather is her frame; it governs patience with the long campaign.
> 8. [choice]The long way, practicing — or the short way, done with the lesson. → Remembered:Her appetite for discipline; the long way becomes habit, or warning.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S19 · [T] · Rose (reflecting)

*Purpose: "Let them print" — said last night, meant now; the line becomes the season's spine.*

> *(Last night: lamplight, the sherry decanter catching light, Letitia's laugh dying mid-note, Augusta's silence doing its arithmetic. She had said it the way one ends an evening —* let them print *— and it had tasted like sherry, brave and warm and slightly reckless, and she had gone to bed feeling like a heroine. This morning it sounds like a* decision*. Not courage, but arithmetic: the paper will come every Thursday whether she is brave or not, so she may as well budget for it the way one budgets for rates.)*
> *(She stands at the window with the string she did not need and says it again, to the empty room. "Let them print." It fits — the way armor fits after the first wearing: strange at the shoulders, then suddenly the only thing shaped like her. She has given the season its spine. Every Thursday the paper will come, and every Thursday she will say it, and the saying will be the war — not the winning of it, but the* waging *of it, which is the part she controls. Augusta has the campaign. Letitia has the comedy. She has the line. It is enough. It has to be enough.)*
> *(She tests the line the way one tests a floorboard — for give, for sound, for rot. Three words that refuse the whole negotiation: no denial, no defense, no arrangement, only the open door and the lifted chin. She says it once more, quietly, to the empty room, which does not applaud. The saying is the war. The war is the saying. The line holds.)*

> *(It is, she realizes, the first wholly hers thing in the campaign. Augusta supplied the strategy, Letitia the comedy, Quill the law — all of them necessary, all of them borrowed. The line is hers: three words, said first over sherry as a flourish, and now re-said in daylight as a policy. She wonders what it will cost her by the twentieth Thursday, the thirtieth — whether a line can wear thin like a coin, whether she will still mean it when the novelty of war has worn off and only the Thursdays remain. The floorboard holds under her testing. It will have to hold under the town's. "Let them print," she says once more, and goes down to breakfast.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh "not courage, but arithmetic" — the line's cost per Thursday. → Remembered:Her unsentimental accounting of defiance; it keeps her honest.
> 2. [stance]Say it to the empty room — vow, rehearsal, or dare. → Remembered:The line's private register; the household hears whichever she practices.
> 3. [micro]Test it "the way one tests a floorboard" — for give, sound, rot. → Remembered:Her habit of testing resolves; tested lines hold.
> 4. [examine]Parse it — "three words that refuse the whole negotiation." → Remembered:Her understanding of the line's work; she'll wield it precisely.
> 5. [stance]"It is enough. It has to be enough." → Remembered:What she asks the line to carry; when it falters, the game knows what broke.
> 6. [social]Who hears the line's history — Letitia, Augusta, or no one. → Remembered:Whether the spine is hers alone; Letitia's Thursday toast depends on it.
> 7. [choice]Accept the casting — or refuse it. → Remembered:Augusta the campaign, Letitia the comedy, herself the line; they play her parts.
> 8. [examine]Remember the sherry-taste, to measure the distance by morning. → Remembered:Her calibration of pose vs. policy; the distance measures growth.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S20 · [D] · Rose, Letitia

*Purpose: the line, tested — Letitia hears it, and understands what it costs.*

> *(Letitia is arranging flowers she does not need to arrange, which means she has been thinking and intends to disguise it as industry.)*
> "Let them print." — Rose
> "You said that last night, darling." — Letitia (not looking up from the stems)
> "I know. I am saying it again, to see if it still works in daylight." — Rose
> "And does it?" — Letitia
> "It works *differently* in daylight. Last night it was a line — said at evening's end, with lamplight on it. This morning it is a *schedule*, Aunt. Every Thursday, the paper. Every morning, the being-mentioned. Every ball, the verdict. I shall have to mean it the way one means a marriage vow — repeatedly, and on purpose, whether one feels like it or not."
> *(Letitia sets down the shears. The flippancy goes out of her like air from a cushion, and what remains is something Rose sees rarely: her aunt being entirely serious, which is always slightly alarming, like a dog doing arithmetic.)*
> "Then I shall remind you of it," — Letitia "— every Thursday, till you are sick of my voice. It is the least an aunt can do. It is also the *most*. I am decorative, darling. You are the general. Decorative people do not win wars, but we *can* hold the line — 'let them print,' every Thursday, like a toast."
> "We are both generals." — Rose "Yours is simply a more comfortable uniform."
> "And a more becoming one." — Letitia "Puce becomes me, darling; strategy does not. But a toast I can manage. Now — the bookseller. Did he bow lower?"
> "He bowed lower." — Rose "The town is revising, Aunt. One bow at a time."*
> *(One bow at a time. Letitia picks up the shears again, and the industry resumes, but the toast has been drunk — silently, between them, in daylight — and Rose understands that her aunt has just enlisted. Decorative people hold lines too. Someone has to pour the wine.)*

> *(She studies her aunt across the flowers — the puce gown, the competent hands, the flippancy that is really a kind of courage with better manners. "Puce becomes me; strategy does not" — and yet here is Letitia, enlisting for a Thursday toast, holding a line she claims no aptitude for. Rose feels something loosen in her chest, something that has been tight since the paper arrived. The town revises one bow at a time. The household, it turns out, revises faster — over flowers, in daylight, with a toast not yet drunk but already promised.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [stance]Test it "in daylight" — experiment, confession, or performance. → Remembered:Why she voices the line; the motive shapes its reception.
> 2. [examine]Weigh the marriage-vow comparison — solemnity, repetition, or trap. → Remembered:Which part she keeps; it defines her discipline's character.
> 3. [social]Accept Letitia's Thursday reminder — gratefully, warily, or with conditions. → Remembered:Whether her aunt holds the line with her; ritual or burden.
> 4. [stance]"We are both generals" — meant, flattered, or half-corrected. → Remembered:The commission she gives Letitia; the aunt's war record starts here.
> 5. [examine]Hear "puce becomes me; strategy does not." → Remembered:Self-knowledge, self-limitation, or deflection; she'll staff her aunt accordingly.
> 6. [micro]File "he bowed lower" — the town's revision, one bow at a time. → Remembered:Her ledger of small verdicts; the bows accumulate into evidence.
> 7. [choice]Tell Letitia of the bookseller's ceremony — or keep the field to herself. → Remembered:What she shares; Letitia's counsel needs intelligence.
> 8. [social]"Like a toast" — drink to it now, or save it for Thursday. → Remembered:Whether the line becomes ritual tonight; rituals need a first keeping.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S21 · [C] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the first morning of the libel — the ritual. Direction + occasion brief, ~20 micro-decisions, story morsels between pins, reveal coda. *

> **Occasion brief (published):** "The first morning of the libel. The dress code: you will be seen — in the Park, at morning calls, in the bookseller's — by a town that read about you over breakfast. The gown must be *unimpeachable*: nothing to hide, everything to show. Mourning colors forbidden; gloves expected. **Editorial criterion: integrity.**"

> **Direction:** the first morning's gown. The occasion is not a ball and not a courtroom — it is the town's first look, the Park at noon, the bookseller's at three, every eye in London doing its own arithmetic. The direction is chosen first, and the twenty pins follow the direction. Three readings of the same brief; one will be hers. The ritual is long — the gown's whole argument, decided piece by piece — and the length is the point: the town read her over breakfast, and now it gets to read her in person, and the reading must be managed.

> **A. The Unimpeachable** — correct in every particular, the armor of a woman with nothing to conceal. Motif: none — the plainness is the point, the cloth speaking without ornament. Thread: grey silk (the plain statement) / silver-grey (the statement, lit) / ivory whitework at the collar only (the one permitted softness). Colorway: Hartwell grey, dove grey, charcoal. Accessories: the plain pendant; grey kid gloves; a simple tortoiseshell hair comb; walking half-boots, sensible.

> **B. The Hartwell** — the Park's palette, sober and landed — the estate, worn as evidence. Motif: oak leaves, worked small at the hem — the land's own botany, the restoration's argument in thread. Thread: bronze silk (the land, stated) / moss-green (the land, living) / dull gold (the land, held). Colorway: deep lichen green, fawn, stone grey. Accessories: the estate brooch, the small oak-leaf one; leather gloves; stout walking boots; no fan — the Park is not a ballroom.

> **C. The Quiet Defiance** — one perfect detail, and let them find it — the statement, whispered. Motif: a single scarlet stitch, hidden — at the hem's underside, the cuff's turn-back, the throat's ribbon knot. Thread: pearl-grey silk (the whisper's ground) / one scarlet thread (the whisper itself). Colorway: pearl grey, dove, the single scarlet note. Accessories: the scarlet ribbon at the throat — the one perfect detail; plain grey gloves; the tortoiseshell comb; half-boots. Let them find it.

> **Decomposed steps:** twenty-one pins — the direction, then twenty five-coin pins, the gown's whole argument, decided piece by piece. Re-picks are free, now and after — the wardrobe keeps every combination, and the choosing can be revisited without cost. No Crowns touch this ritual; the dressing is the player's own, bought with play.

> 1. [choice]**Direction — the commitment:** A, B, or C — the morning's reading, chosen first, the pins to follow. → Remembered: the direction; the town gets its first look, managed.

> 2. [micro]**Pin 1 — the silhouette:** the columnar line, plain and upright; or the fuller skirt, the season's. 5 coins. → Remembered: the skirt's argument; the column does not apologize.

> 3. [micro]**Pin 2 — the bodice's cut:** high and plain, the unimpeachable line; or the softer drape. 5 coins. → Remembered: the bodice's honesty; the high cut concedes nothing.

> 4. [micro]**Pin 3 — the neckline's finish:** the plain collar, the small ruffle, the bare throat with the ribbon. 5 coins. → Remembered: the throat's framing; the collar is the direction's signature.

> *(Story morsel: Letitia through the door — the fitting half-done, the pins in the maid's cushion — and stops, and looks, and says it: "Powder, not paint, darling. The town is watching for* paint *— the rouged cheek, the guilty flush manufactured. Give them nothing to find." She touches Rose's cheek with one finger, critically, the way she touches pastry. "Good. You look furious, which is honest. Honest is allowed.")*

> 5. [micro]**Pin 4 — the sleeves:** long and plain, the working morning; or the lace at the wrist. 5 coins. → Remembered: the arm's statement; plain sleeves say the business, not the ornament.

> 6. [micro]**Pin 5 — the waist's emphasis:** the natural line, the sash, the corseted plain. 5 coins. → Remembered: the waist's logic; the natural line is the unimpeachable's own.

> 7. [micro]**Pin 6 — the skirt's length:** the street's practical, clearing the pavement; or the longer sweep. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hem's arithmetic; the Park is walked, not processed through.

> 8. [micro]**Pin 7 — the fabric's weight:** the wool that holds its line all day; or the silk that softens by noon. 5 coins. → Remembered: the cloth's discipline; the first morning is long, and the gown must outlast it.

> *(Story morsel: the maid, doing up the back — the buttons, the small ones — and her voice comes over Rose's shoulder, low, the intelligence service making its morning report: "The Park is* full *of it, miss. Mrs. Almeria Fane read it aloud — at the bench by the water, miss, to six ladies, with* expressions*." The buttons keep going up, one by one. "Thank you," Rose says, and means it, and files Mrs. Almeria Fane under* readers to watch*.)*

> 9. [micro]**Pin 8 — the color's depth:** the saturated grey, the pale dove, the charcoal's gravity. 5 coins. → Remembered: the color's courage; saturation is a statement, pallor a question.

> 10. [micro]**Pin 9 — the grey's temperature:** the cool grey, the blue cast; never the warm, never the near-white. 5 coins. → Remembered: Augusta's edict, honored — no white; the withholding is the wardrobe's law till vindication.

> 11. [micro]**Pin 10 — the trim:** none, the plainest; or the self-trim, the same cloth speaking quietly. 5 coins. → Remembered: the trim's restraint; the unimpeachable does not plead by ornament.

> 12. [micro]**Pin 11 — the closure:** the long line of buttons up the back; or the side's convenience. 5 coins. → Remembered: the back's honesty; the long line is the gown's spine, and the maid's morning work.

> *(Story morsel: the note, slipped under the door — Augusta's hand, the writing like a fence. "No white. White is for vindication, and we are not vindicated yet. — A." Rose reads it twice, standing in her half-pinned gown, and feels the edict settle over the fitting like a second garment. No white. Not the collar, not the gloves, not the handkerchief's edge. She nods at the note, though no one is there to see it, and chooses the dove's coolest cast. The withholding, worn.)*

> 13. [micro]**Pin 12 — the hem's finish:** the plain hem, the weighted; the pavement's dust considered. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hem's pragmatism; the town's streets are not ballrooms.

> 14. [micro]**Pin 13 — the glove:** the wrist's neat kid; or the longer, the more formal. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hand's formality; gloves are expected, and the expectation is honored.

> 15. [micro]**Pin 14 — the shoe:** the walking half-boot's sense; or the slipper's delicacy. 5 coins. → Remembered: the foot's pragmatism; the morning is walked, the Park is long.

> 16. [micro]**Pin 15 — the hair:** the dressed, the simple knot, the comb's placement. 5 coins. → Remembered: the head's composure; the simple knot is the gown's roof, unshowy.

> *(Story morsel: the* Lantern's *second paragraph — remembered mid-fitting, uninvited, the phrase about the suitors swimming up out of the cheap type. She sets it aside the way one sets aside a pin that pricks: noted, not seized, put down carefully where it cannot draw blood. The maid watches her face and says nothing, which is the maid's own courtesy. The fitting continues. The paragraph waits in the drawer with the first number. It can wait. Everything can wait except the gown.)*

> 17. [micro]**Pin 16 — the bonnet:** the brim's width, the trim's restraint, the strings tied or loose. 5 coins. → Remembered: the bonnet's framing; the face must be seen, unshadowed, unbowed.

> 18. [micro]**Pin 17 — the wrap:** the spencer's neatness; or the shawl's softness; or none, the nerve of the bare shoulder in morning air. 5 coins. → Remembered: the shoulder's answer; the spencer is armor, the bare shoulder is argument.

> 19. [micro]**Pin 18 — the reticule:** the small, the discreet, the nothing-flashy. 5 coins. → Remembered: the reticule's discretion; it carries a handkerchief, a coin for the crossing-sweeper, nothing else.

> 20. [micro]**Pin 19 — the handkerchief:** the plain, the monogrammed — the initials, stated or withheld. 5 coins. → Remembered: the handkerchief's plainness; even the linen declines to plead.

> 21. [micro]**Pin 20 — the single detail:** the defiance's whisper — the scarlet stitch's placement, the ribbon's knot, the one thing they will find if they look close. 5 coins. → Remembered: the whisper's location; the statement, placed where only the attentive will find it.

> *(Reveal coda: the gown, complete — the twenty pins, the direction, the whole argument made cloth. The maid steps back. The glass gives her back a Hartwell: grey, plain, upright, the single detail waiting like a held breath. "Nothing to hide," Rose says to the glass, "everything to show." The glass does not argue. She takes up the gloves — expected, honored — and goes down to a town that read about her over breakfast. Let it read her in person now. The reading is managed, pin by pin, choice by choice.)*

> **Jewels (owned pieces only):** the ritual's jewel shelf draws on pieces the player already owns — the shelf itself is open at any time, never a ritual step. Simple, morning-appropriate pieces — the plain pendant, the tortoiseshell hair comb; the Gazette judges occasion-inappropriate jewels, and the morning's criterion is integrity.

> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [examine]Re-read "No white" — weigh what the withholding asks. → Remembered:Her reading of the note; it governs color choices till the verdict.
> 2. [stance]Take "powder, not paint" — wisdom, fussing, or warning. → Remembered:How she takes the aunt's fieldcraft; it sets their register.
> 3. [examine]Mark the maid's news — Fane read it aloud; learn the readers. → Remembered:Her map of London's opinion-makers; Fane bears watching.
> 4. [micro]Set the Lantern's paragraph aside "like a pin that pricks" — or don't. → Remembered:Discipline with intrusive text; it serves Thursdays.
> 5. [choice]Note which direction her hand reaches for first. → Remembered:Her instinct; it colors the outcome.
> 6. [examine]Weigh pendant against comb — what each says under judgment. → Remembered:Her jewel grammar; she learns the Gazette's language.
> 7. [stance]Wear "nothing to hide, everything to show" — armor, truth, or dare. → Remembered:The spirit she brings; the town reads it first.
*Animation: Shared (shared ritual presentation).*

### L1.S22 · [T] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the mirror — the reveal coda; the unimpeachable, worn.*

> *(The mirror moment: grey morning light, the gown settling — correct in every particular, memorable in none, which is precisely the point. Twenty-one decisions, and the result is a dress no one will describe. She sees not a girl defying a libel but a woman going about her morning: the deeper defiance, the kind that does not know it is defiance and cannot be accused of it. Augusta's note sits on the dressing table, handwriting exact, initial like a seal:* No white. White is for vindication, and vindication is not this morning's business. *White is the verdict's color. Today she wears* endurance *— the greys of a woman with nothing to prove, the blues of a morning that asks nothing.)*
> *(She turns; the skirt moves as a well-cut skirt moves, without argument. She thinks of the* Lantern's *second paragraph — the mortgages to the pound, the foreign coin — and sets it aside, like a pin that pricks. Pins prick. One removes them, and goes on dressing. That is the whole of the ritual's teaching, if it taught anything: the prick is information; the dressing is the answer.)*
> *(She folds Augusta's note into the drawer with the paper — "No white" filed beside the libel, the verdict's color waiting its turn. The gloves go on last. She is not beautiful this morning; she notes it with interest. She is* believable*, which is rarer and more useful — beauty is an assertion, and assertions can be argued with. Believability simply continues. She squares her shoulders the way Augusta squares hers, and goes down.)*

> *(The mirror gives her back the whole of it without comment: the grey that asks for nothing, the blue that promises nothing, the gloves, the shoulders squared in imitation of a woman three decades older. She looks, she decides, like someone's competent governess — and then she smiles, because a competent governess is exactly what the* Lantern *cannot use. Pyke needs a heroine or a harlot; he has arranged for both. What he has got is a woman going about her morning. Let him arrange that. The gloves are on. The woman in the glass nods, once, and goes down.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Check the gown's forgettability — "memorable in none" — as feature. → Remembered:Her grasp of the brief's paradox; her standard for campaign dress.
> 2. [stance]Receive "believable, rarer and more useful" — pride, relief, or mourning. → Remembered:What she asks fashion to do now; the atelier's briefs follow.
> 3. [micro]File "No white" with the libel — the verdict's color waiting. → Remembered:The drawer becomes the war's archive; what she files, she'll answer.
> 4. [examine]The gloves going on last — the ritual's final seal. → Remembered:Her dressing discipline; the order of things becomes armor.
> 5. [social]Square her shoulders "the way Augusta squares hers." → Remembered:Whose bearing she wears; the household sees the general in it.
> 6. [stance]Trust "the defiance that does not know it is defiance." → Remembered:Her theory of the campaign's deepest move; it governs appearances.
> 7. [examine]Study the mirror's verdict on her face. → Remembered:Her honest read; the mirror becomes a counsel she consults.
> 8. [choice]Judge the reveal: the ritual worked, was theater, or is beginning. → Remembered:Her faith in the machinery; it shapes the next dressing.

### L1.S23 · [T] · Rose, Letitia, Augusta

*Purpose: the descent — the household sees her; the campaign's first inspection, passed.*

> *(The stairs. The hall below, very quiet. Letitia has stopped fussing with the shawl — Augusta stilled her with a look some minutes ago — and now there is only the sound of Rose's shoes on the steps, and the household holding its breath the way households do when the inspection is also a verdict.)*
> "Well." — Augusta "Unimpeachable."
> "That was the brief, Grandmama." — Rose
> "The brief was *integrity*, child. You have interpreted it as armor." — Augusta "I approve. Armor is what integrity looks like under attack. When the war is over, you may interpret it softer. Not before."
> *(Armor is what integrity looks like under attack. Rose keeps that — it is the kind of sentence that will be useful later, quoted back at exactly the right moment, probably by Augusta herself.)*
> "You look," — Letitia "— like a woman who has never heard of the *London Lantern*, and does not intend to start. Like a woman whose mornings contain only toast and correspondence."
> "I have heard of it, Aunt." — Rose "I simply decline to be *dressed* by it. Pyke may print what he likes. He may not choose my gown."
> *(Letitia fetches the shawl and fusses until Augusta stills her with a look. The hall is very quiet; the London house's mirror flatters no one — it gives back the gown, the gloves, the woman going about her morning, with the indifference of a good witness. Rose sees herself, and beside her the general and the aunt: the whole of her army. Three women. One war. "Shall we?" she says. "We shall," says Augusta. And they go out — the regiment of three — into the London morning.)*

> *(The regiment of three crosses the hall — Augusta first, Rose between, Letitia last with the shawl folded over her arm like a flag of truce. The street door opens onto the London morning, grey and ordinary and entirely unconcerned with penny papers. Rose steps out first, because someone must, and feels the town's gaze settle on her like weather — present, impersonal, endurable. She has practiced this. The walk carries her. Behind her, Augusta's step is measured; Letitia's is already quickening with the pleasure of being looked at. Three women. One war. The campaign's first inspection, passed — and the town, watching the procession, revising.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [stance]Receive "Unimpeachable" — verdict, beginning, or burden. → Remembered:How she takes the general's approval; it sets her hunger for the next.
> 2. [examine]Keep "armor is what integrity looks like under attack." → Remembered:Her working definition of integrity-at-war; the Gazette will judge her by it.
> 3. [stance]Deliver "he may not choose my gown" — defiance, wit, or doctrine. → Remembered:The line's register; the household quotes it when fashion is battlefield.
> 4. [social]Meet Letitia's shawl-fussing — endure, stop, or thank her. → Remembered:How she handles the aunt's nerves; fussing is love with nowhere to go.
> 5. [examine]Take the unflattering mirror's verdict — or dismiss the glass. → Remembered:Whether she trusts indifferent witnesses; the mirror becomes counsel.
> 6. [micro]Say "shall we?" — the one ordering, or the one asking. → Remembered:Who commands the regiment of three; the tone decides it.
> 7. [choice]Walk out first, between them, or last. → Remembered:Her place in the formation; the town reads the procession.
> 8. [stance]Feel "three women, one war" — smallness, or sufficiency. → Remembered:Her faith in the household as army; it sustains her in thin weeks.

### L1.S24 · [T] · London (observed)

*Purpose: the morning calls — the gown, seen; the town's verdict, first installment.*

> *(She pays her morning calls, and she is* seen*. At Lady Drummond's, by Mrs. Almeria Fane — who read the libel aloud at breakfast, Rose is reliably informed, doing all the voices, and now studies the gown like a customs inspection, eyes moving hem to collar to glove, searching the seams for the swindle. Let her search. The seams are honest; the inspection will find only dressmaking. By Lady Drummond herself, who receives her exactly as usual — the same tea, the same quarter-hour, the same warmth — which is the whole of the verdict: nothing has changed, therefore nothing in the paper can be true, therefore good morning.)*
> *(By two young men in the hall who stop talking as she passes and resume, quieter, when she has gone — the small surrenders of the town, one lowered voice at a time. At the Pritchards' it is the same. Nothing happens. That is the whole of it — nothing happens, and the nothing is* enormous*: a town that has read the libel and looked at the girl and found the two irreconcilable. The gown has spoken before she opened her mouth. That is the whole of fashion's power — not decoration, but testimony, given in wool and cut.)*
> *(On the way home she passes the newspaper boy. He touches his cap. She buys a second copy of the paper she has already read — another penny for the same libel — and he looks at her as if she has done something brave. Perhaps buying the paper that libels you twice, in the open, in daylight, is the first small victory: proof the libel is a thing she* handles*, not a thing that handles her. She folds it under her arm next to the string. Two pennies. Her own name, twice purchased. The town is revising.)*

> *(At home she puts the second copy in the drawer with the first — two pennies' worth of libel, filed like ammunition, or like evidence, or like the beginning of an archive she has not yet named. The drawer sticks, then gives. She thinks of Fane's inspection, Lady Drummond's quarter-hour, the boy's cap touched in daylight, and understands that the morning has been a kind of trial — the ton's court in session, herself the evidence, the verdict delivered in lowered voices and unchanged tea. Acquitted daily, in public. Augusta's arithmetic holds. The gown goes back on its peg, having spoken. She has not yet opened her mouth today, and already she has won the morning.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Study Fane's "customs inspection" — what the chief reader is revising. → Remembered:Her read of Fane's revision; the woman becomes London's bellwether.
> 2. [stance]Receive the enormous nothing — victory, suspense, or warning. → Remembered:How she reads silence; it governs patience with quiet weeks.
> 3. [social]The two young men, quieter after she passes — note it, or let it pass. → Remembered:Her attention to small surrenders; they accumulate into verdicts.
> 4. [examine]Read Lady Drummond's "exactly as usual" — the verdict inside the reception. → Remembered:What normalcy costs a hostess; she files it as courage.
> 5. [micro]The second penny — spent deliberately, or quietly. → Remembered:Whether she performs the handling; the street reads performance.
> 6. [choice]Stay the full call — or cut it short. → Remembered:Whether she completes the town's rituals under fire; London watches the clock.
> 7. [stance]Trust "the gown has spoken" — or distrust the ease. → Remembered:How much she lets fashion carry; the atelier's briefs follow.
> 8. [examine]Price the nothing — what composure cost this morning. → Remembered:Her accounting of composure; it prices every appearance after.

### L1.S25 · plot beat (romance: Octavia) · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: Octavia's letter — the studio, steady ground; the one room where Rose never performs.*

> *(The afternoon post brings Octavia's letter — her hand unmistakable, letters formed like brushstrokes; the studio's frank; written, by the date, the morning the* Lantern *printed. Octavia wrote it before the town had finished reading, which means she wrote it the moment she knew — pen faster than gossip, for once.)*
> *("My dear Rose — I write not to comfort you but to tell you the studio is unchanged. The light is the same. The sitters come. There is a room in London where you are not a paragraph, and it is mine, and you are welcome whenever the being-mentioned grows tiresome. — O." Rose reads it twice — the second time slower, the way one re-reads kindness, to make sure it was really there. Then she sits down, all at once, as if the letter were a chair.)*
> *(The studio. The one room where she never performs. She had not known how much of her life is performance — the gown, the walk, the face for morning calls, the line for the empty room. All of it, necessary; all of it, costing. And Octavia, who paints faces for a living, offering the one room where no face is required. It is the most tactful thing anyone has done all week, and Octavia has done it without leaving her studio.)*
> *(She writes back at once. "Thank you. I shall come. Not yet — first I must be unimpeachable in public. But I shall come. — R." She seals it. Steady ground, held in reserve — the way generals hold reserves: not spent early, not spent cheaply, there when the fighting is hardest.)*
> *(She carries the letter upstairs and puts it in the drawer with the* Lantern *— Octavia's steadiness filed beside Pyke's malice, the two Londons side by side. The drawer is becoming an archive of the war: the enemy's dispatch, the friend's reserve, the verdict's color waiting. She closes it gently. Some drawers hold cutlery. Hers holds the campaign.)*

> *(Upstairs, the drawer closes on both Londons — Pyke's malice and Octavia's steadiness, filed side by side like specimens. She stands a moment with her hand on the drawer front, thinking of the studio's north light and the smell of linseed, of a room where no face is required and no paragraph follows her in. Not yet, she told Octavia. First the public unimpeachability, the campaign, the Thursdays. But the reserve is there — held, not spent — and reserves, as Augusta would say, win wars. She goes downstairs steadier than she went up, which is the whole of what the letter was for.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh "you are not a paragraph" — mercy, and cost. → Remembered:What the line gives her; her measure of every kindness.
> 2. [stance]Permit the collapse — "all at once" — or catch herself. → Remembered:Her honesty about weariness; a pressure valve.
> 3. [choice]Ration the studio with "not yet" — or set its date. → Remembered:When she allows the reserve; the timing matters.
> 4. [social]Pitch the reply — gratitude, steadiness, or confession. → Remembered:What she shows Octavia; the painter reads ink.
> 5. [examine]File Octavia's letter with the Lantern — instinct or design. → Remembered:The drawer's evolution; filed together, answered together.
> 6. [stance]"All of it, performance; all of it, necessary." → Remembered:Her reckoning; it shadows every public hour.
> 7. [micro]Read it twice — the second reading slower. → Remembered:Her habit of re-reading kindness; what she needs most.
> 8. [choice]Tell Augusta of the offer — or keep the reserve. → Remembered:Whether it stays hers alone; maps have no off-map rooms — or do they.

### L1.S26 · [D] · Rose (reflecting)

*Purpose: the letter, answered — what Octavia's steadiness costs, and gives.*

> *(She thinks about Octavia as she seals the letter — the studio's north light, the smell of linseed, sitters who pay in coin and sit very still and reveal themselves by increments; Octavia's way of looking at a face until the face gives up pretending. It occurs to Rose that she would not care to sit for Octavia just now — there are weeks when one does not want to be looked at until the looking stops — and then she laughs at herself, quietly, because that is exactly why the room matters: it is the one place where nobody is painting her.)*
> *(What she turns over like a coin: "you do not require comforting." Nobody else has said it. The truest thing said today, and the kindest — because it is the only sentence that treats her as intact. The others will comfort her: Letitia will distract, Augusta will command, Quill will advise. Each kindness in its kind. Octavia alone offers what she needs — not a plan, not a weapon, a* room*.)*
> *(She puts the sealed letter on the tray. Some alliances are declarations — announced, toasted, minuted. This one is a* reserve *— and reserves, as Augusta would say, win wars: not by fighting early, but by being there when the fighting is hardest. Letitia's kindness carries. Augusta's commands. Octavia's does neither. She thinks of doors left open — the studio door, in London, standing open. She will walk through it one day. The thought steadies her more than any plan. Some alliances are trumpets. This one is a door.)*

> *(She seals the letter and weighs it in her hand — light, after Quill's heavy express paper, after the* Lantern's *damp pennyworth. Some papers are weapons and some are rooms. She thinks of the sitters, revealing themselves by increments under Octavia's patient eye, and wonders what increments of herself she would reveal if she ever sat still long enough — then sets the thought aside, because the campaign does not allow stillness, and that is exactly why the reserve matters. The tray waits by the door. The letter goes on it. A door, held open, in London, in reserve.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Test "you do not require comforting" — truest today, and is it true. → Remembered:Whether she accepts the premise; it shapes what she asks of others.
> 2. [stance]Sort the kindnesses — carries, commands, neither. → Remembered:Her taxonomy of love; she'll lean on each kind differently.
> 3. [examine]Consider the sitters "revealing themselves by increments." → Remembered:What she fears Octavia's eye would find; it waits for her visit.
> 4. [choice]Adopt "reserves win wars" — or keep the studio outside strategy. → Remembered:Whether the friendship is militarized; the game remembers.
> 5. [micro]Picture the door "standing open" — do open doors frighten her. → Remembered:Her relationship to refuge; it governs her eventual visit.
> 6. [stance]Keep "some alliances are trumpets; this one is a door." → Remembered:Whether the line enters her vocabulary; kept lines return.
> 7. [social]Tell Octavia what the letter did — or keep the collapse private. → Remembered:What she confesses; Octavia's replies answer her honesty.
> 8. [examine]Weigh what the studio costs Octavia — and what Rose owes. → Remembered:Her ledger of the friendship; she will repay it.

### L1.S27 · [T] · Rose, a footman

*Purpose: Quill's letter arrives — the solicitor's seal; the law's shadow enters.*

> "From Mr. Quill, miss. By express." — Footman (the letter is heavy; Quill's seal — the Hartwell solicitor's, the family's legal shadow for two generations; the footman holds the salver the way one holds a live thing, carefully, at a slight distance)
> *(Rose weighs it in her hand. Quill does not write express about nothing — the man bills by the interview and prefers his counsel delivered sitting down, across a desk, with the clock running. An express letter means he could not wait for any of that. She breaks the seal standing — sitting for Quill's letters has never helped; they are read on one's feet, the way one receives bad news from a doctor. "Miss Hartwell — private — by express." Private. By express. The law's shadow, entering the London house.)*
> *(She unfolds it. Heavy paper, faintly of the office — twenty years of Hartwell business: mortgages, settlements, the engineered ruin's paper trail, all of it smelling faintly of sealing wax and caution. Express letters from Quill are rare as comets; he prefers the billable interview. An express letter means he could not wait. She thinks of the last one — the Rackham business, two years ago, which Augusta opened standing and read an hour without sitting, the one that began the unmaking of everything. The hall is very quiet. She has known Quill since she was a girl — since he explained settlements to her with diagrams, patient as a tutor. Whatever is in this letter could not wait. She reads.)*

> *(The footman waits — salver still at that careful distance, as if the letter might detonate. In a sense it already has: the household has gone very quiet, the way houses do when the law enters them. Below stairs they will be reading the salver the way she reads faces — express, private, the solicitor's seal — and drawing their own conclusions, correctly. Rose is aware of the stairs, the doors, all the listening architecture of the London house, and chooses — deliberately — to read it here, in the hall, standing, rather than carrying it upstairs to privacy. Quill wrote to her, not to the general. Let the house see her receive it standing, the way Augusta received the Rackham letter: on her feet, for an hour, without sitting. She unfolds the heavy paper, and reads.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]The salver "held like a live thing" — the household's fear, at a glance. → Remembered:How staff read legal shadows; who below stairs holds law.
> 2. [stance]Break the seal standing — ritual, defiance, or habit. → Remembered:Her posture toward bad news; the house knows where she stands.
> 3. [examine]Weigh "private — by express" before reading. → Remembered:Her discipline in not rushing; Augusta would approve the pause.
> 4. [micro]Remember the Rackham letter — Augusta standing an hour. → Remembered:What she carries from the unmaking; the parallel steadies or frightens.
> 5. [social]Read it alone — or send for Augusta first. → Remembered:A private hour with the law; Quill wrote to her, not the general.
> 6. [examine]Smell the office — twenty years of Hartwell business. → Remembered:Her ledger with solicitors; it tunes how she hears Quill.
> 7. [stance]"Known Quill since she was a girl" — trust, wariness, or both. → Remembered:Her baseline; the letter is read through it.
> 8. [choice]Prepare for the worst, hope for the best, or refuse to rehearse. → Remembered:Her discipline of expectation; it determines how the letter lands.

### L1.S28 · [D] · Rose (alone, reading)

*Purpose: Quill's counsel — the law's shape; what a libel action is, and is not.*

> *("My dear Miss Hartwell — I have read the* Lantern's *number. You will ask what the law can do. The law can do this: a libel action is a weapon, but the court is a stage, and stages have audiences. Sue now, and we fight on ground of our choosing, evidence ready — mortgages, settlements, the engineered ruin's paper trail, all filed and waiting. Sue later, and we fight with more evidence on ground Pyke has poisoned. Sue never, and we fight with conduct alone — the slowest weapon, and in some hands the surest. There is no wrong answer. Choose the ground, Miss Hartwell. The law will hold whichever ground you choose. — Quill." She reads it three times — once for the argument, once for the warnings underneath the argument, once for what he did not write. What stays is "the court is a stage." She thinks of Augusta: "the ton is the jury, and the jury is always in session." Two stages. One war. Two audiences, both hungry.)*
> *(She folds the letter — crease, crease, exact, the way Quill himself would fold it, which makes her smile despite everything. Quill will call tomorrow. She puts the letter in the drawer, with the* Lantern *— the solicitor's counsel filed beside the enemy's dispatch, the two Londons side by side again. The drawer is becoming crowded. The war is fought increasingly in drawers: paper against paper, filing against filing, the whole campaign conducted in a sticking drawer in the London house. She closes it. Tomorrow, the solicitor in person.)*

> *(She reads the letter a fourth time — or rather, she re-reads the re-readings, checking what she has taken from it the way Quill would check a brief. "There is no wrong answer." She distrusts that sentence the way she distrusts all comfortable sentences; there is always a wrong answer, only it is not marked. Sue now: ground of her choosing, evidence ready — but the reading aloud, the reporting, the months. Sue later: more evidence, poisoned ground. Sue never: conduct alone, the slowest weapon, and in some hands the surest — whose hands are hers? She does not know yet. The drawer takes the letter. The war, increasingly, is fought in drawers.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Set "the court is a stage" beside "the jury is always in session." → Remembered:Her comparative strategy; S9 is argued on exactly this ground.
> 2. [stance]Receive "there is no wrong answer" — relief, suspicion, irritation. → Remembered:What she asks of counsel; Quill learns whether she wants judgment or cover.
> 3. [choice]Read it three times — the third for what she missed, or comfort. → Remembered:Her study habits; thoroughness becomes her legal signature.
> 4. [examine]Audit the ready evidence — and test its completeness. → Remembered:Her private audit; gaps found now can be filled before any writ.
> 5. [social]What Augusta hears tonight — the letter, the counsel, or nothing till Quill comes. → Remembered:How she stages tomorrow's interview, tonight.
> 6. [micro]Fold it "crease, crease, exact" — learn it, or mock it gently. → Remembered:Whether she adopts Quill's exactness; it marks her correspondence.
> 7. [stance]Meet "the war is fought increasingly in drawers." → Remembered:Her feeling about the paper war; it colors how she tends the drawer.
> 8. [examine]Use the night before counsel — questions, ground, or herself. → Remembered:How she uses the night; the interview's yield depends on it.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S29 · [D] · Rose, Mr. Quill

*Purpose: Quill arrives — the solicitor in person; the law's shape, discussed.*

> *(He comes at eleven, as promised — hat in hand before he is fully through the door, which tells her everything about the night he has had.)*
> "Miss Hartwell." — Quill (in the sitting room, hat in hand, looking exactly like a man who has sat up all night with a brief — the candles, the papers, written in the lines around his eyes) "You have read the number."
> "I have read it, filed it, and been mentioned because of it at morning calls." — Rose (gesturing him to sit — she has learned, from Augusta, that solicitors think better sitting down) "Tell me the shape of an answer, Mr. Quill. Not the answer — the *shape*. You wrote that the court is a stage. Tell me about the stage."
> *(He sits. The hat goes on his knee. Twenty years of Hartwell business settle into the chair with him.)*
> "The shape is this." — Quill "A libel action — Hartwell against the *Lantern* — is won or lost on three questions. One: is it false? The ruin-as-fraud. Two: was it printed with malice? Pyke prints *everything* with malice." He pauses. "Three — " He looks at her. "— three is the question no solicitor likes to ask aloud, and I ask it because I have served this family twenty years and I owe you the asking. Three: can you bear to have every true thing in it read aloud in court? Every mortgage. Every debt. Every figure, to the pound. In open court. Reported in every paper in London."
> *(The third question lands like a writ. Rose feels it itemize her — grandfather's ledgers, father's hand, the Park's figures — each true thing a small detonation, each to be read aloud by a clerk with no interest in her composure. She does not answer at once. Quill, who has served this family twenty years, does not rush her. That, she thinks, is what twenty years buys: a solicitor who knows when the silence is the client thinking, and when it is the client drowning.)*

> *(She does not answer the third question — not yet. It is the kind of question that should be carried, not answered on the spot, like a live coal. Instead she memorizes the catechism the way she memorized her catechism as a girl: false, malice, unbearable. One, two, three. She will recite it to Lavinia when the time comes, she thinks, and then wonders why Lavinia — of all people — is the audience she pictures. The hat remains on his knee, forgotten. Mortal matters. She offers him tea, because solicitors think better sitting down, and think best of all with tea.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]The lines around his eyes — the night's work; what the reading cost. → Remembered:Rose measures counsel's devotion; loyalty priced in sleepless miles.
> 2. [social]Seat him — "solicitors think better sitting down." → Remembered:Whose lesson she's using; Augusta's fieldcraft in her hands.
> 3. [stance]Ask for "the shape, not the answer." → Remembered:How she questions counsel; Quill shapes his advice to the asking.
> 4. [examine]Weigh the third question before answering it. → Remembered:Her estimate of the unbearable; it shadows the S9 decision.
> 5. [choice]Answer the third question now — or hold it for the choosing. → Remembered:Whether she tests herself early; the game remembers her claim.
> 6. [social]Receive "I owe you the asking" — twenty years inside it. → Remembered:Her reckoning with his loyalty; it weights his counsel.
> 7. [examine]Note "Pyke prints everything with malice" — habit, not passion. → Remembered:Her theory of motive; habitual malice is starved differently than hatred.
> 8. [micro]Memorize the three questions — false, malice, unbearable. → Remembered:The law's catechism; she'll recite it to Lavinia.

*Animation: Shared.*

### L1.S30 · [D] · Rose, Quill

*Purpose: the third question — what a trial costs the plaintiff; the true things, priced.*

> "Every true thing." — Rose
> "Your grandfather's speculation — true. Your father's debts — true, and documented in his own hand, which is the worst kind of true. The mortgages — true, to the pound." — Quill (leaning forward, the hat forgotten) "A trial will read them aloud, one by one, in open court, and the *Lantern* will print the trial, and the town will read the trial, and we will have paid for the privilege of being libeled *twice*: once by Pyke, once by the court record."
> *(Libeled twice — and the second libeling itemized on a solicitor's bill. Rose does the arithmetic: the ruin-as-fraud is false in arrangement and true in every brick, and a courtroom would lay the bricks out one by one for the town's inspection. The town that has just learned to bow lower would learn, instead, the figures to the pound. It is a price. Everything is a price this week.)*
> "And if we win?" — Rose
> "If we win, the court says the arrangement was false — the *braiding*, as your aunt would call it. The damages will be substantial." — Quill "Pyke cannot pay substantial damages. The *Lantern*, Miss Hartwell, would become *yours* — or nothing. That is the prize." He pauses. "I served this family twenty years. I told your father what I tell you: the law is a weapon, but a *heavy* one, and it wounds the hand that holds it. It wounds *before* it strikes — in the reading aloud, the reporting, the months of it. Choose the ground. I will hold it. But choose knowing the weight of what you lift."*
> *(The* Lantern *— hers. Or nothing. Rose turns the prize over: Pyke's press, silenced by ownership, the gutter bought and closed like a bad debt. It is a pretty fantasy and she knows it for one — the kind of thought one has at midnight and does not mention at breakfast. The hat, forgotten on his knee. When Quill drops formality, the matter is mortal. She chooses to remember that signal. "Choose the ground," he said. "I will hold it." Twenty years, holding. She will not waste the holding.)*

> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Face "documented in his own hand, the worst kind of true." → Remembered:Her reckoning with her father's debts; the documents exist.
> 2. [stance]Receive "libeled twice" — steadiness, anger, or bleak humor. → Remembered:Her stomach for the plaintiff's price; it governs the writ.
> 3. [examine]Weigh the prize — "the Lantern would become yours." → Remembered:Whether she covets the weapon; the fantasy returns at season's end.
> 4. [choice]Accept "wounds before it strikes" — or refuse the weapon. → Remembered:Her doctrine of legal violence; Quill will hold her to it.
> 5. [social]Hear "I told your father, too late to matter." → Remembered:What Quill carries from the last ruin; she won't waste his warning.
> 6. [stance]Receive "choose the ground; I will hold it." → Remembered:The compact; it governs every instruction she gives him.
> 7. [micro]The forgotten hat — formality dropped, matter mortal. → Remembered:When Quill drops formality it's mortal; she'll know the signal.
> 8. [examine]Weigh "in some hands the surest" — and ask whose hands are hers. → Remembered:Her self-knowledge as instrument; it shapes which road she walks.

### L1.S31 · [T] · Rose (reflecting)

*Purpose: the heavy weapon — what it means to sue; the plaintiff's price.*
> *(She walks Quill to the door and stands in the doorway after, thinking about heavy weapons. To sue is to stand in open court and hear the true things read aloud — the speculation, the debts, the theft, all of it entered into the record in a barrister's voice — to pay for being libeled twice, as Quill said, once for the printing and once for the unprinting. It is also the only answer that* ends *anything. Conduct starves the libel, slowly, week by week. Denials feed it — every denial is a second helping. A verdict* kills *it. She thinks of Augusta: "the ton is the jury." She thinks of Quill: "the court is a stage." Two stages. One war. The paper is still in the drawer. The drawer is still closed — sticking, crowded, patient. She has not yet decided. That is honest, and honesty is the policy now. What she* has *decided: win in the open, buy no judge. The rest is tempo.)*
> *(Quill's hands shook — very slightly — on his hat, there at the door. Twenty years the family's solicitor, and frightened: not for himself, for her. The most honest thing said all day, and it was not said in words. She thinks about what it means to be the thing a careful man is frightened for. It means the danger is real — not the paper's damp kind of real, but the kind that makes a solicitor's hands shake on his hat. She stands in the doorway a moment longer, the street quiet around her, the lamps not yet lit. Then she goes back inside without deciding. The doorway has done its work. Decisions ripen in doorways; this one is not ripe yet.)*
> *(Inside, the house is settling toward evening — the kitchen sounds, Letitia humming something tuneless upstairs, Augusta's pen scratching in the morning room. The ordinary sounds of a household at war footing, which turn out to be exactly the ordinary sounds of a household. She thinks about the triptych she made at the door: conduct starves, denials feed, a verdict kills. Three verbs. It has the neatness of a drill command, and like a drill command it conceals the cost of obeying it. Starving takes weeks. Killing takes a courtroom. Feeding is what happens if she does nothing on purpose. She files the triptych with the rest — the penny, the smudge, the knock, the nose, the dog, the rented press — and goes in to dress for dinner. The file is getting thick. It is only Thursday.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Test the triptych — "conduct starves; denials feed; a verdict kills." → Remembered:Her strategic grammar; the three verbs become shorthand.
> 2. [stance]Honor "not yet decided" as honesty — or chafe at it. → Remembered:Her discipline with open questions; no bad deciding just to be done.
> 3. [micro]File Quill's hands shaking, very slightly, on his hat. → Remembered:The human cost of counsel; she'll remember who was frightened for her.
> 4. [examine]Read the drawer — "sticking, crowded, patient." → Remembered:The archive's personality; she tends it like a living thing.
> 5. [choice]Define "the rest is tempo" — when, not whether. → Remembered:Her sense of timing; the season's turnings are tempo decisions.
> 6. [stance]Meet "the only answer that ends anything." → Remembered:Her hunger for an ending; it pulls in the long middle weeks.
> 7. [social]Walk him to the door herself. → Remembered:How she honors counsel; devotion answers the respect she's shown.
> 8. [examine]Stand in the doorway after — the street's quiet as counsel. → Remembered:Her threshold pause; decisions ripen in doorways.

### L1.S32 · [D] · Rose, Augusta

*Purpose: Quill's visit, reported — the general weighs the heavy weapon.*
> "Quill says the law is a heavy weapon." — Rose
> "Quill is right, and Quill is cautious, and both are virtues in a solicitor." — Augusta "What did you tell him?"
> "Nothing. I told him I would choose the ground." — Rose
> "Good." — Augusta "A solicitor told the answer too quickly stops thinking." She straightens — the general, shifting to the campaign map. "Now — Thursday we are for Bath. The *Lantern* is printed here, read here, believed here. London is the blast, child. Bath is the planning. The ton, the clubs, the coffee-houses — the war's geography changes, and we change with it."
> "And Lavinia?" — Rose
> "Lavinia," — Augusta "— will come. I have known Lavinia Crane since she was in short coats, and watched her hate Pyke for a decade with a patience that would do credit to a general." A pause, weighted. "She has waited for Pyke to give her a reason not merely personal. When she comes — " Another pause. "— listen to her aim, Rose. Never her reins. Her aim is Pyke, broken. Her reins are Lavinia's, and Lavinia's reins have led better women than you into interesting ditches."
> "I shall listen to the aim." — Rose
> "See that you do." — Augusta "And child — the heavy weapon. Quill is right about the weight. But a heavy weapon, lifted at the right moment, ends the war in a single stroke."
> *(Rose sits with that — the single stroke, the right moment — and thinks about doorways, and ripening, and the not-yet. Augusta has just given her permission to keep the weapon on the table without lifting it. It is the most useful thing anyone has said about the lawsuit all day. She nods, once, the way Augusta nods, and goes to tell Letitia about Bath. The geography is changing. The trunks will have to come down.)*
> *(She finds Letitia in the morning room, re-reading the* Lantern's *second paragraph as if it might have improved since breakfast. It has not. "Bath," Rose says, and her aunt looks up. "Thursday. The blast here, the planning there." Letitia folds the paper with something like relief. "Bath," she repeats. "The waters, the gossip, the excellent shops. And Lavinia Crane, presumably, circling." She rises, already halfway to the bell-pull. "I shall pack the puce wrapper. One must have something violent in Bath.")*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]File "a solicitor told the answer too quickly stops thinking." → Remembered:Her management of counsel; she'll ration answers to keep Quill thinking.
> 2. [stance]Receive "London is the blast; Bath is the planning." → Remembered:How she meets escalation; the war only grows from here.
> 3. [examine]Weigh "hate Pyke for a decade with a general's patience." → Remembered:Her measure of Lavinia; patience as devotion or obsession.
> 4. [choice]Take "listen to her aim, never her reins." → Remembered:Her doctrine for Lavinia; the Ch2 alliance is negotiated on this line.
> 5. [examine]Picture the "interesting ditches" — and their cost before her. → Remembered:Her caution's vividness; the ditches stay in view.
> 6. [social]Pledge "I shall listen to the aim." → Remembered:What she promises the general; Augusta holds her to the words.
> 7. [stance]Meet "ends the war in a single stroke." → Remembered:Her appetite for the decisive blow; it pulls against patience.
> 8. [micro]Note "since she was in short coats." → Remembered:The general's deep knowledge; the alliance has history Rose doesn't own.

### L1.S33 · [T] · Rose, Letitia

*Purpose: packing for Bath — the campaign moves; the war's geography changes.*
> *(The house fills with the chaos of packing — trunks open like mouths, hatboxes, tissue paper everywhere, the particular uproar of a household decamping. Letitia's embroidery (the lopsided dog, unfinished) is packed like a relic, wrapped in tissue with a reverence the dog does not merit but receives anyway. Augusta's packing is done by others, as Augusta's everything is done by others — she stands in the middle of it all giving orders in the tone of voice she uses for battles. Rose packs the drawer-paper at the bottom of her trunk, under the pelisse. Evidence, or ammunition; she has not decided. Perhaps both — the best evidence usually is. The paper lies there, damp no longer, flattened by the drawer, looking smaller than it did at breakfast. It is still the enemy. It is just — packed.)*
> *(Letitia packs the puce wrapper — "for courage, darling; one must have something violent in Bath" — and Rose, watching, feels the morning's whole war folding itself into canvas and leather. The grey dress, the scarlet ribbon, the twenty-one pins: all of it going into trunks, all of it traveling. The maid watches everything with enormous eyes and will tell the whole street the Hartwells have gone to Bath to fight the paper. Let her. Gossip is the gutter's currency, and the Hartwells are about to spend it — carefully, deliberately, the way one spends foreign coin. Rose catches the girl's eye and smiles, and the girl smiles back, startled. The intelligence service, bought with a smile. Cheap at the price.)*
> *(On the last evening they dine early and quietly. Letitia reads aloud from a novel — something with a vicar and a misunderstanding, the literary equivalent of gruel. Nobody mentions Pyke. It is a truce, and truces are also strategy: the household's nerves, rested for the road. In the morning the carriage comes, and London recedes — the square, the Park, the boy's bell, all of it going backwards. Rose does not look back. Generals do not look back at battlefields they mean to retake. Besides — she pats the trunk, where the paper lies under the pelisse — she is bringing the war with her. It rides better packed.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Decide what the drawer-paper is — evidence, ammunition, or both. → Remembered:The paper's status; it determines how she uses the archive.
> 2. [social]Let the maid tell the street — gossip spent as currency. → Remembered:Her first spend of the gutter's coin; the talk becomes an asset.
> 3. [micro]Pack the puce wrapper "for courage." → Remembered:Whether she carries her aunt's courage; the wrapper returns in London.
> 4. [stance]Not looking back — discipline, superstition, or performance. → Remembered:Her ritual of departure; the campaign's custom.
> 5. [examine]The lopsided dog "packed like a relic." → Remembered:Her eye for what her people love; what they're fighting for.
> 6. [choice]Keep the last evening's truce — or break it with planning. → Remembered:Rest or council; the household's nerves answer.
> 7. [social]Settle the bill — the first headquarters struck. → Remembered:How she closes a headquarters; the ritual repeats.
> 8. [examine]Pat the trunk — the paper under the pelisse, through the leather. → Remembered:The enemy's text travels with her; generals carry maps.

### L1.S34 · [F] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the road outfit — chosen; practical, unimpeachable, nothing to hide; posting-inn scrutiny, road-worn muslin, arrival readiness.*
> *(The trunk is packed; the drawer-paper lies at the bottom, under the pelisse. What remains is the road — six hours, two changes of horses, the posting inns, the inn yards full of eyes. Augusta's rule: a lady is never off-duty on the road. The posting inn is the most democratic stage in England — ostlers, coachmen, travelers, all watching, all carrying what they see to the next town. What does a woman wear to be watched by strangers — and then to arrive in Bath, where the whole town will have heard the carriage, ready? Not the fine morning gown: too fine for the road, and road-dust on fine muslin reads as carelessness. Not the invisible gown: invisibility reads as hiding, and she has nothing to hide. The answer is the third thing, as it so often is.)*
> "The road has its own arithmetic. Six hours, two changes, every eye in the yard — and Bath at the end of it, already listening for the wheels." — Rose (laying out the candidates on the bed)
*Choices (no coin cost; the game remembers):*
- **The road-worn muslin** — plain, perfect, proven; traveled and arrived before. *(Readiness+; Augusta approves.)*
- **The severe merino** — darker, plainer; the road as discipline, the arrival as statement. *(Resolve+; Letitia mourns the muslin.)*
- **The best pelisse over the working dress** — armor for the inns, ease underneath for the arrival. *(Pragmatism+; the maid approves the strategy.)*
> *(She chooses — the hand going to one of the three the way it went to the direction this morning, the instinct noted and filed. The dress comes out; the others go back. The maid looks at the choice, and understands: this is not packing. This is strategy. "The road-worn," the girl says quietly, "miss. It has arrived before." Rose looks at her — the intelligence service, offering counsel now — and nods. "It has arrived before," she agrees. "Let us hope it remembers how." The muslin goes into the traveling case. The paper goes under the pelisse. Nothing to hide — and everything ready.)*
> *(She stands a moment with the traveling case packed and thinks about the posting inns — the yards, the ostlers, the travelers eating chop dinners and watching everything. Six hours of being watched by strangers, and then Bath, where the whole town will have heard the carriage. Augusta's rule holds: a lady is never off-duty on the road. But off-duty was never the aim. The aim is the arrival — stepping down looking exactly like a woman who has traveled six hours and is ready for six more. The muslin has arrived before. It remembers how. She closes the case.)*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh the posting inn as a stage — who watches, and what they carry on. → Remembered:Her literacy in road scrutiny; it serves her.
> 2. [examine]Reject the fine morning gown — road-dust as carelessness. → Remembered:What she refuses the road; the refusal defines the brief.
> 3. [examine]Reject the invisible gown — hiding, when she has nothing to hide. → Remembered:The tactic retired for the road; daylight is policy now.
> 4. [stance]Define "arrival readiness" — what Bath's first impression requires. → Remembered:Her standard; Bath is judged by it.
> 5. [micro]Lay the dress out — the maid reading strategy in muslin. → Remembered:The laying-out; below stairs learns the campaign.
> 6. [choice]Choose — muslin, merino, or pelisse; the hand's decision. → Remembered:What she wore on the Bath road; the remembered choice.
> 7. [examine]Test "nothing to hide" against the paper in the trunk. → Remembered:The drawer-paper travels too; the irony is filed.

### L1.S35 · [T] · Rose, Letitia, Augusta

*Purpose: arrival in Bath — the lodgings, the town watching; dress, composure, and timing as social performance.*
> *(The carriage turns into the street and Bath does what Bath does: it watches. Curtains move — actually move, the fabric twitching — a maid stops sweeping a step and stares, a chair-man pauses mid-stride. By the time the wheels stop, the news is traveling faster than the horses: the Hartwells are come, the girl from the* Lantern*, come to Bath. Rose does not alight at once. Augusta's rule: an arrival is a performance, and performances have timing. She lets the footman hand down Augusta first — the general, surveyed by the street, surveying it back — then Letitia, then herself. The traveling dress has done its work: she steps down looking exactly like a woman who has traveled six hours and is ready for six more — practical, unimpeachable, nothing to hide.)*
> *(The landlady curtsies — deeply, the curtsy of a woman who has read the* Lantern *and decided to be kind about it, or at least to be* paid *about it. The lodgings are aired, the drawing room ready, the card tray already waiting, expectant as a dog. Rose unpacks the trunk herself — pelisse, gowns, tissue by tissue, the ritual of settling — and at the bottom, the folded* Lantern*. She puts it in the drawer of the Bath desk. The drawer does not stick — it slides smooth as water, which seems, absurdly, like an omen. London's drawer stuck; Bath's glides. The war has a second headquarters now, and the furniture is cooperating. She stands at the Bath window and feels the campaign settle like a gown being pinned: the being-seen, the being-mentioned, the Thursdays.)*
> *(Outside, a chair stops. Another card for the tray — the tray is filling faster than the trunks emptied. The town is already watching, already calling, already doing its arithmetic. Let it watch. She arrived ready to be seen — the road-worn muslin, the composure, the timing, the whole of the first impression, delivered. She touches the trunk, where the paper lies in its new drawer, and nods at it. New city. Same war. The headquarters is open for business.)*
> *(Letitia comes in as the last trunk is closed, takes one look at the smooth-gliding drawer, and nods as if it had spoken. "London's stuck," she says. "Bath's glides. I consider this* significant*." Rose considers telling her that furniture is not an oracle, and decides against it. The household needs its omens. She has the grey dress, the ribbon, the paper in its new drawer. It is enough for an arrival. The rest — the cards, the calls, the watching — can begin.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Read the watching street — curtains, the maid, news outrunning horses. → Remembered:Her first Bath reading; the town's speed becomes data.
> 2. [stance]Time the alighting — Augusta's rule of arrival. → Remembered:Her command of timing; arrivals are performances.
> 3. [examine]Audit the traveling dress — six hours, and arrival-ready. → Remembered:Whether the [F] choice delivered; the game holds her to it.
> 4. [social]Receive the landlady — the lodgings' first verdict. → Remembered:How she meets new households; they become her infrastructure.
> 5. [micro]Unpack the trunk herself — tissue by tissue. → Remembered:What her hands need in a new city; the unpacking is her settling rite.
> 6. [examine]The drawer "which does not stick" — omen, or refuse omens. → Remembered:Whether she takes signs; the habit follows her into the campaign.
> 7. [stance]Stand at the Bath window — the campaign settling. → Remembered:The window becomes her council chamber; decisions ripen there.
> 8. [choice]"Let it watch" — set her first week's tempo. → Remembered:Her pace with Bath's curiosity; interest as asset or siege.
