### L1.S36 · [D] · Rose, Letitia

*Purpose: the evening post — cards, letters, the town's first moves; the war's social weather.*
> "Cards." — Letitia (coming in with the tray, flushed — the evening post has clearly been *interesting*) "Six. Mrs. Almeria Fane — *again*; she has pursued us from London to Bath like a particularly well-dressed Nemesis — the Pritchards, Lady Drummond's 'at home' — oh, and this." She holds up a card between two fingers, the way one holds a dead mouse. "No name I know. Heavy stock. Engraved. Left by hand, not ten minutes since. The footman says a woman in scarlet left it. *Scarlet*, darling."
> "Read it." — Rose
> "'Miss Lavinia Crane presents her compliments to Miss Hartwell —'" Letitia reads, and stops. Turns the card over. Reads the back. Looks up, and her face has done something complicated. "Oh, my love. You must read the back yourself. I cannot."
> *(Rose takes the card. It is heavy — heavier than correspondence needs to be, heavy the way Augusta's cards are heavy: the weight is the message. She turns it over. The handwriting on the back is — she will think about the handwriting later; first, the fact of it. A card, left by hand, by a woman in scarlet, ten minutes since. Lavinia Crane. Augusta's voice, from thirty years of short coats: she has waited for Pyke to give her a reason not merely personal. Rose holds the card and feels the evening change temperature, the way rooms did this morning. Something has arrived. It is heavy, engraved, and scarlet. She reads the back.)*
> *(The footman hovers — the man who took it from the woman in scarlet, ten minutes since, still half-braced as if the card might require further handling. "She said nothing, miss," he offers. "Just the card. And she looked — " He stops, the professional discretion of the well-trained warring with the human urge to describe a woman in scarlet. "She looked like she knew the house, miss." Rose considers this. Lavinia Crane, knowing the house — or wanting it thought that she does. Either way, the card has done its first work before being read. She dismisses him with thanks, and a coin for the trouble, and turns the card over in hands that are, she notes with interest, perfectly steady.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]The six cards as intelligence — a map of the town's curiosity. → Remembered:Her first London reading; the cards rank the town's interest.
> 2. [social]Fane "again" — pursued from London; meet the Nemesis. → Remembered:Her handling of Fane; the woman's verdict matters in two cities.
> 3. [examine]Weigh "heavy stock, engraved" — the card's weight as message. → Remembered:Her literacy in stationery as signal; it serves all season.
> 4. [micro]Picture "a woman in scarlet" — what scarlet means. → Remembered:Her first image of Lavinia; the scarlet stays in the portrait.
> 5. [stance]Letitia holding it "like a dead mouse." → Remembered:How she manages the aunt's nerves; composure is her responsibility too.
> 6. [choice]Command "read it" — or take the card at once. → Remembered:Her command style with intelligence; reporting follows reception.
> 7. [examine]Honor "you must read the back yourself." → Remembered:Whether she spares her aunt the hard parts; the boundary is set here.
> 8. [social]Who is present for the back — Letitia stays, or is sent away. → Remembered:Witnesses become confidants; choose them.

### L1.S37 · [D] · Rose (alone, reading)

*Purpose: Lavinia's card — the gutter guide, offering herself; the handwriting, weighed.*
> *("You need a gutter guide, darling. I am one. The* Lantern *printed filth about my family when I was nineteen, and I have hated Pyke with a clean, professional hatred ever since — the kind that keeps accounts, darling, not the kind that throws things. He has now printed filth about yours. I propose an alliance — of information, not affection. Come alone, or not at all. — L.C." Rose reads it three times. "Clean, professional hatred" stays with her — the most honest thing offered since the libel printed, honest the way a drawn knife is honest. She thinks of Augusta: "listen to her aim, never her reins." She thinks of the* Lantern's *second paragraph and wonders what it printed about the Cranes, when Lavinia was nineteen — what filth, what arrangement, what title. She turns the card over. Heavy stock. Engraved. A name like a gauntlet.)*
> *(She holds the card to the light. Everything about Lavinia Crane is* deliberate *— the heavy stock, the engraving, the hand-delivery, the scarlet. "Come alone, or not at all." The condition interests her more than the offer: Lavinia wants no audience, no witnesses, no Augusta. Someone who can decide — alone, unwatched, unadvised. Rose wonders what the reins look like, on a woman like this; wonders whether the interesting ditches have Lavinia's wheel-marks in them already. The Cranes' filth, at nineteen — she will ask, one day. Not yet. First, the reply. Four lines. By hand, tonight. The handwriting on the card is extraordinary — controlled to the point of chill — and she studies it the way she studied the* Lantern's *paragraph breaks this morning: where the enemy pauses for breath. Only Lavinia is not the enemy. Not yet. Possibly not ever. The card does not say.)*
> *(Letitia has gone very quiet in the corner — the aunt, watching, the dead-mouse fingers now folded. "Well," Letitia says at last, "she writes a letter." It is the faintest praise, and therefore the most sincere. Rose almost smiles. She puts the card down — carefully, the way one puts down something that might be loaded — and goes to the desk. The reply will be four lines. She does not yet know which four. The choosing, she is learning, is mostly waiting: for the fourth reading, for the stillness, for the general's arithmetic to finish. She takes up a pen. The pen is steady. That, at least, is decided.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh "clean, professional hatred" — its honesty, and its chill. → Remembered:Her first measure of Lavinia; the alliance's epigraph.
> 2. [examine]Study the "extraordinary" handwriting. → Remembered:Her reading of Lavinia's control; it warns her what she's dealing with.
> 3. [stance]Receive "information, not affection." → Remembered:The terms of the alliance; affection later would breach them.
> 4. [examine]Parse "come alone, or not at all." → Remembered:What the condition tests; she answers it at the key decision.
> 5. [social]The Cranes' filth at nineteen — ask now, later, or never. → Remembered:Whether she seeks Lavinia's wound; asking has a price.
> 6. [micro]Think of Letitia's burning letter — the parallel. → Remembered:The parallel steadies her; burned letters are a Hartwell tradition she may break.
> 7. [choice]Name it — gauntlet, invitation, or trap — before deciding. → Remembered:Her hypotheses; the S38 decision is argued between them.
> 8. [examine]Test "someone who can decide" — is she that someone yet. → Remembered:Her self-audit before the alliance; it sets the reply's tone.

### L1.S38 · plot beat · Rose, Letitia

*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — LAVINIA'S CARD.*
> *(Letitia watches her read, and understands — in the instantaneous way of aunts — that this card matters more than the six others combined. The six were curiosity. This is* intent*. Rose reads it a fourth time, and Letitia, watching her niece's face, sees the general doing arithmetic: the aim, the reins, the ditches, the decade of patience. The room is very quiet. The card sits on the desk, heavy stock, engraved, scarlet by association. Four lines of reply, by hand, tonight — or a polite refusal — or the third thing, the meeting that is neither. Rose puts the card down and picks it up again, which tells Letitia everything about the state of the arithmetic.)*
> "Well, darling? The gutter guide offers herself. What does the general say?" — Letitia (gently, for once without the comedy)
> *(Rose thinks of the two readings of the card — gauntlet, invitation, trap — and the third reading she has not named yet, the one that says* weapon*. She thinks of Augusta's arithmetic: listen to the aim, never the reins. She thinks of the* Lantern's *filth about the Cranes, at nineteen, and what nineteen years of clean, professional hatred might know that she needs. The alliance is not really about Lavinia, she realizes — it never was. It is about what kind of general she intends to be: the kind who fights alone out of pride, keeping her hands clean and her intelligence thin, or the kind who uses every weapon the war provides, even the ones in scarlet. She looks up. Letitia raises her eyebrows — the whole question, in one gesture. The choice waits, patient as the drawer.)*
> *(And then, underneath the arithmetic, the simpler thought — the one she will not say to Letitia, the one she barely says to herself. She is* curious*. About the Cranes' filth at nineteen. About the clean, professional hatred, and what it has learned in all these years. About the woman in scarlet who knows the house. Curiosity is not a general's virtue, but it is a Hartwell's habit, and she has stopped pretending otherwise. She picks up the pen. Four lines, by hand, tonight — or the polite refusal, or the single meeting. The pen waits. The card waits. Letitia's eyebrows wait. She writes.)*
*★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — Lavinia's card:*
- **Accept the guide** — *Lavinia+; the alliance begins.* Rose writes back at once: she will come, and she will listen. *(Augusta: "Her aim, child. Never her reins.")*
- **Decline her** — *Independence+; Lavinia intrigued.* A polite refusal — Rose fights her own war, with her own weapons. *(Lavinia will not stay declined; the card was an opening, not an offer.)*
- **Meet her first, alone** — *Information+; caution kept.* One meeting, no commitments — Rose will hear what Lavinia knows before deciding what Lavinia is. *(Letitia approves the prudence; Augusta approves the nerve.)*
> *(The game remembers the choice — and the means: the open alliance, the proud refusal, the cautious single meeting. The destination was never the question. The signature is hers.)*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [stance]Feel the card's weight against the six others — the arithmetic Letitia sees. → Remembered:How she ranks intelligence; the household learns what she values.
> 2. [examine]Face the real question — "what kind of general she intends to be." → Remembered:Her self-definition; the alliance decision becomes precedent for every ally after.
> 3. [social]Answer "what does the general say?" — claim the title, deflect it, or earn it. → Remembered:Whether she accepts the generalship aloud; titles accepted become duties.
> 4. [examine]Weigh "fights alone out of pride" — and name the pride honestly. → Remembered:Her reckoning with pride; it is the enemy inside the war council.
> 5. [micro]Read it a fourth time — and know what the fourth reading is for. → Remembered:Her thoroughness with offers; Lavinia will be answered with full attention.
> 6. [choice]Consult Augusta before answering — or decide alone, as the card demands. → Remembered:Whether "come alone" extends to the choosing; the general's counsel is sought or set aside.
> 7. [stance]Meet "the choice waits" — with appetite, dread, or calm. → Remembered:Her posture toward deciding; it becomes her signature at every key decision.

### L1.S39 · Gazette sting

*Purpose: Bell's verdict on the libel's arrival — contempt, precisely scored; openly Bell's.*
> *("The* Lantern *prints. The Gazette has no verdict for ink this cheap — yet." — Mrs. Nance Bell, proprietor, in her own column, named from the first edition.)*
> *(Rose reads it at breakfast — the* Gazette *comes with the morning post, as it always has, the heavy paper, the clean type, the whole of it a rebuke to the* Lantern's *damp thinness just by existing. She reads Bell's paragraph twice, and then she laughs — properly laughs, for the first time in three days, the sound startling the breakfast table. "Ink this cheap." It is the exact phrase she needed and could not have written herself: contempt, but* precise *contempt — the kind that costs the contemner nothing and the contemned everything. Bell has aimed it, in public, at Pyke. Not at her. At* him*. She feels the paragraph land like reinforcements arriving: the town's unbought judge, speaking, on the record, in her week.)*
> *(She reads the "yet" three times. It is the whole of the paragraph's second meaning, and it is aimed at* her*: the* Gazette *will judge, when there is something worth judging, and the something will have to be her — unimpeachable, unbowed, week after week, until the judgment is not a kindness but a* recognition*. The "yet" is a standard, and a deadline, and a dare, all in three letters. She takes Letitia's sewing scissors — the aunt surrenders them without a word, which for Letitia is the equivalent of a speech — and cuts the paragraph out, and files it in the drawer with the* Lantern*. Cheap ink below; heavy judgment above. One of them is hers to answer. The other is hers to earn. Augusta says nothing, and the eggs — for the first time all week — are eaten hot.)*
> *(She keeps the cutting — the paragraph, the scissors' neat work — and holds it up to the light the way she held the* Lantern *to the light on the first morning. Heavy paper. Clean type. The unbought judge, speaking. Three days ago she stood at a window and said "let them print" into the lamplight, and it tasted like sherry. This morning Bell has said her own version of it — printed it, signed it with the proprietor's name, aimed it at Pyke. The town has its verdict on the* Lantern*. Hers is still to earn. She puts the cutting in the drawer, on top of the* Lantern*, where she will see it every Thursday. The "yet," watching her. Good. Let it watch.)*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [stance]Laugh "for the first time in three days." → Remembered:What loosens her; the household knows she's still in the war.
> 2. [examine]Read the "yet" three times. → Remembered:Her understanding; the "yet" becomes her deadline.
> 3. [micro]Cut the paragraph out — Letitia's sewing scissors. → Remembered:The archive's second founding; the scissors become its instrument.
> 4. [examine]Study "contempt, precisely scored." → Remembered:Her education in the Gazette's register; she'll recognize its judgments.
> 5. [stance]Receive "one of them is hers to earn." → Remembered:How she carries owed verdicts; the debt drives the campaign.
> 6. [social]Take Letitia's silence — "the equivalent of a speech." → Remembered:The aunt's eloquent silences; Rose learns to hear them.
> 7. [micro]Eat the eggs hot — "for the first time all week." → Remembered:Small normalcies as victories; the hot eggs join the ledger.
*Animation: Shared (sting card).*

### L1.S40 · cliffhanger · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the card, answered — and Thursday, coming; the gutter guide waits.*
> *(She writes the reply at her Bath desk. The reply is four lines — brevity as discipline, Lavinia reads brevity as clearly as words. She does not show it to Letitia, or to Augusta. She seals it. She rings for the footman. "By hand, tonight. Miss Lavinia Crane." The footman takes the salver and goes, and she stands at the window and watches him go down the street, the sealed answer going with him. Somewhere out there Lavinia Crane waits for an answer to a card written in a hand too precise to be casual. Somewhere else — in an office over a press in Grub Street — Ambrose Pyke is setting next Thursday's number. The paper prints on Thursdays. The rented press turns in the small hours. Rose Hartwell watches the Bath dusk come down and thinks, deliberately, the line she has carried all week:* let them print*.)*
> *(What the four lines say is between her and the scarlet woman. She watches the footman turn the corner and does not follow him with her eyes any further — the choosing is done, the tempo set. Below, the lamps are being lit one by one; ahead, the Thursdays, fifty-two of them, fifty-two pennies, fifty-two arrangements. "Let them print," she says to the darkening street, aloud, and means it — the daylight version, the policy version, the one with witnesses, even if the only witness is Bath. The gutter guide waits. The press turns. The Thursdays come. She stands at the window until the dusk is done, and then she goes in to dinner. The season — the real season — is underway.)*
> *(In the hall, Letitia is waiting — pretending not to, which fools no one. "Well?" she says. Rose considers the four sealed lines, the footman, the scarlet woman, the press in Grub Street, the fifty-two Thursdays. "It is done," she says. "Whatever it is, it is done." Letitia studies her face, and nods, and does not ask what the lines said — the boundary, honored. They go in to dinner together. Behind them, the Bath dusk finishes its work, and the lamps burn, and somewhere the press turns, and the Thursdays come.)*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [micro]Write four lines — brevity as discipline. → Remembered:Her epistolary discipline; Lavinia reads brevity as clearly as words.
> 2. [social]Show it to no one — keep the reply hers alone. → Remembered:Whether she guards the alliance's beginning; secrecy costs solitude.
> 3. [examine]Read "a hand too precise to be casual" once more before answering. → Remembered:Her final assessment; the precision is honesty or performance.
> 4. [stance]Order "by hand, tonight" — as command, eagerness, or strategy. → Remembered:The tempo she sets; Lavinia answers at the speed she's given.
> 5. [examine]Picture Pyke "setting tomorrow's number" — the enemy at work. → Remembered:Her sense of the parallel war; it shortens any savoring.
> 6. [stance]Say "let them print" to the darkening street — aloud, and meant. → Remembered:The line spoken to Bath; the city is its witness now.
> 7. [choice]Mark the threshold — "the real season begins" — or refuse ceremony. → Remembered:How she marks beginnings; the war's calendar starts here.

## Chapter 2 — "The Gutter's Geography"

**Occasion:** Lavinia's Bath; coffee-houses, hawkers' corners, the Pump Room's whispers. **Preparation ritual:** the coffee-house morning — plain but perfect; be nobody, see everything. **Purse shelf:** shillings for hawkers' information; pennies for coffee.

**What changes for Rose:** She learns where the *Lantern* travels — the four hands, one night, from press to street. She names Lavinia's agenda aloud and prices the alliance. She hears her own name sold for a penny, and buys the name of the man who sells it.

### L2.S1 · [D] · Rose, Lavinia Crane

*Purpose: the meeting — Lavinia, the gutter guide; where the Lantern travels.*

> *(Lavinia Crane receives her in a scarlet room, which is to say a room the color of a warning. Morning light comes through tall windows and lands on a great deal of gilt. Lavinia herself is scarlet too — a wrapper, a shawl, something with too much drape for ten o'clock — and she does not rise. "Darling," she says, the way other women say good morning. "You came alone. How brave. Or how well-advised.")*
> *("My grandmother advises," Rose says, and sits, because Lavinia has gestured and one does not stand on ceremony with a woman in scarlet. "I decide." Lavinia rings for chocolate. The bell is answered with a speed that suggests councils disguised as social calls are customary in this house — the tray arrives before the sentence is finished, the chocolate already frothed, the cups already warm. Rose files the speed away. A household that serves chocolate this fast is a household accustomed to receiving intelligence with it.)*
> *("You want to know where the Lantern travels." Lavinia takes her cup black. "The ton does not go to the gutter, darling — it merely reads what the gutter sends up, like a weather report from a country one never visits. It is printed in Fleet Street — Pyke's office, over the press, rooms smelling of damp and ink and other people's money. It goes down by the night mail coach — the bundles tossed at the inn yard by dawn, Bath's share of London's filth, with the coal and the parcels. By morning it is in the hawkers' hands — boys crying it on Milsom Street for a penny, paid by the stack, extra for the loud corners, the loudest corners to the loudest boys. By seven it is on the coffee-house tables — free, darling, free, left like bait, because a free paper is read twice and talked about three times. By noon it is in the Pump Room — the back benches, the lowered voices, 'have you seen' — and by dinner it is conversation." She sets down her cup. "And conversation, darling, is the only press that matters.")*
> *(Rose does not take notes. She memorizes instead, the way Augusta taught her — press, hawker, coffee-house, Pump Room. Four hands. One night, and a mail coach. That is the gutter's geography, the Bath branch of it. That is the road her name travels, every Thursday, while she sits in Bath being unimpeachable and drinking her chocolate. She finishes the cup. It is excellent chocolate. She distrusts it anyway.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [stance]Deliver "my grandmother advises; I decide." → Remembered:How she presents the partnership; Lavinia deals accordingly.
> 2. [examine]The chocolate's speed — councils disguised as social calls. → Remembered:Her read of the establishment; the alliance has infrastructure.
> 3. [micro]Memorize "press, hawker, coffee-house, Pump Room." → Remembered:The four-hands map; she'll walk it from S7.
> 4. [examine]Weigh "a free paper is read twice." → Remembered:The gutter's economics; it shapes her counter.
> 5. [examine]Test "conversation is the only press that matters." → Remembered:The final battlefield; target conversation, not print.
> 6. [stance]Hear "while we sat in Bath being unimpeachable." → Remembered:Let Lavinia devalue Bath, or defend the first phase.
> 7. [social]Answer "how brave, or how well-advised." → Remembered:How she meets Lavinia's tests; always testing.
> 8. [choice]Take notes, or memorize. → Remembered:What the scarlet room sees her write; the guide reads caution.
*Animation: Shared.*

### L2.S2 · [D] · Lavinia, Rose

*Purpose: the agenda, named — the Cranes' old filth; "broken, not bought."*

> *("Why are you telling me this?" Rose asks. It is the first honest question of the morning, and she asks it plainly, because Lavinia respects plainness the way other women respect flattery.)*
> *("Because the Lantern printed filth about my family when I was nineteen." Lavinia pours the chocolate with a steady hand — too steady, the steadiness of long practice. "My mother's — " She stops. The sentence is not finished; it will not be finished. "Pyke arranged it into a habit, darling. A family habit. Inherited, like a nose. Does that sound familiar?" "It sounds verbatim," Rose says, very quietly. "The same arrangement. The same braid. The same tune with different names.")*
> *("It is his only tune. He plays it for whoever pays him — the notes never change, only the names." Lavinia sets down the pot. "I wanted to sue. Three pages, very fine, very furious. My father read it, folded it, and put it in the fire. 'The gutter is not fought from the gutter.' And died unavenged." She says it without self-pity, which is worse. "I have waited years for a reason not merely personal — a reason with standing. He has now printed filth about you — and you, darling, are the first person he has libeled who might actually win.")*
> *(Lavinia leans forward, and the scarlet wrapper shifts, and for a moment the performance drops and there is only the woman underneath it, nineteen and furious and still waiting. "So here is my agenda, named: I want Pyke broken. Not bought, not sued into politeness — broken. His paper discredited, his press silent, his name a joke in the clubs where he is now a prophet. If that is also your aim, we are allies." She sits back. The glitter returns, on schedule. "If it is not — " A shrug, beautifully done. "— the chocolate is still excellent.")*
> *(Rose studies the shrug — the practiced ease of it, ten years in the rehearsal. The hatred underneath is the real thing; the shrug is only the scar tissue over it, worn smooth by a decade of handling. She files both: the hatred, to be used; the shrug, to be watched.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]The stopped sentence — "my mother's — " → Remembered:The wound she won't name; filed for the day trust extends.
> 2. [stance]Say "it sounds verbatim" very quietly. → Remembered:Proof she's not the first; lineage steadies or burdens.
> 3. [examine]Weigh "he plays it for whoever pays him." → Remembered:The payer question; the season's central mystery.
> 4. [choice]Meet "broken, not bought." → Remembered:How far she'll go; the alliance's violence calibrated here.
> 5. [examine]Count the burned letters — three fires. → Remembered:Father, Letitia's, Lavinia's; Rose may not burn hers.
> 6. [social]Receive "you might actually win." → Remembered:What Lavinia needs; the alliance's asymmetry named.
> 7. [stance]Read the shrug — "the chocolate is still excellent." → Remembered:Take the no-commitment door, or refuse it.
> 8. [micro]Hold "her aim, never her reins" through it all. → Remembered:Counsel as filter; it sifts everything Lavinia says.

### L2.S3 · plot beat · Rose, Lavinia

*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 1/3 — LAVINIA'S ALLIANCE.*

> *(The agenda, named, sits on the table between them like a third cup. Rose weighs it the way Augusta taught her — what does it cost, what does it buy, who holds the reins. The hatred is clean. That is the thing about it: no tangle, no grievance half-admitted, no performance. A professional hatred, ten years in the making, pointed at exactly the right man. It suits her terms so neatly that it frightens her. Neatness, in allies, usually means one has not yet seen the whole of them.)*
> *(It is also the only thing about Lavinia entirely predictable. The hatred will not change, will not soften, will not be bought off. Everything else — methods, timing, collateral, the hour of the morning she chooses to strike — Lavinia will decide in the moment, beautifully, and Rose will learn of it afterward. Full partners: the scarlet room, the shared table, the war conducted jointly. Use her warily: take the knowledge, the routes, the introductions, and keep the woman at arm's length. Alone: the clean road — no guide, no glitter, no one to blame but herself. The chocolate has gone cold. The room waits.)*
> *(Augusta's voice, in her head, uninvited and exact: her aim, never her reins. Rose looks at Lavinia — the scarlet, the steadiness, the nineteen-year-old fury ten years banked — and understands this is the first decision of the war that is truly hers. Not Augusta's doctrine. Not Lavinia's glitter. Her road, her generalship, her consequences. She sets down the cold cup.)*
*★ KEY DECISION 1/3 — Lavinia's alliance:*
- **Full partners** — *Lavinia+; the alliance sealed.* "Broken, not bought. I agree to the terms, and to the partnership." *(Augusta will warn; Lavinia will exult.)*
- **Use her warily** — *Information+; distance kept.* Take her knowledge, her routes, her introductions — and keep her at arm's length. *(Lavinia, amused: "Wary. How *grandmotherly* of you.")*
- **Keep her at arm's length** — *Independence+; Lavinia intrigued.* Polite, distant, grateful — and alone in the gutter. *(Lavinia: "Alone in the gutter, darling? How *brave*.")*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [stance]Receive "the first decision truly hers." → Remembered:Her ownership of the choosing; the standard for later key decisions.
> 2. [examine]Run Augusta's ledger — cost, price, reins — on each road. → Remembered:Her method survives the general's absence; the ledger is hers now.
> 3. [micro]The cold chocolate — time passing while she decides. → Remembered:Her awareness of decision's duration; Lavinia reads hesitation.
> 4. [examine]Weigh "no one to blame but herself." → Remembered:Her honesty about solitude; the alone road's cost is counted first.
> 5. [social]Consult Augusta in memory — or decide unconsulted. → Remembered:Whether the general's voice is counsel or crutch.
> 6. [stance]Answer beneath the choice — "what kind of general walks what kind of road." → Remembered:Her self-definition; it outlasts the alliance itself.
> 7. [choice]Deliver the decision — at once, after a night, in writing. → Remembered:The delivery's form; Lavinia reads ceremony like cards.
> *(The game remembers the choice — and the means: the partnership sealed, the wary use, the solitary road. Lavinia will deal with whichever woman chose.)*

### L2.S4 · [D] · Rose, Lavinia

*Purpose: the terms of the guide — what Lavinia offers, what she withholds; the alliance's working shape.*

> *("If we are to do this," Rose says, "I need to know what you offer. Precisely. Not hatred — information. Hatred I have in abundance, and it is free." Lavinia laughs — a real laugh, surprised out of her — and folds her hands, the rings settling with a small gilt click.)*
> *("Information, then. One: the hawkers' corners — which boys, which cries, which corners matter. Two: the coffee-houses — which tables take it free, and why free matters, and who reads it aloud, and who laughs, and who laughs because the man beside him laughed first. Three: the Pump Room's back benches — who laughs, who repeats, who believes, and who merely wants to be seen laughing, which in Bath amounts to the same thing. Four: Pyke himself — his hours, his habits, his chapel." She pauses. "What I withhold: my family's file. The old filth, the old invoices, who paid then, and what it cost us. That comes later, darling. Trust is a currency, and I am not rich in it. I was bankrupted young — by the Lantern, at nineteen.")*
> *("Nor am I," Rose says, "where you are concerned. Rich in trust, I mean." "Good." Lavinia is delighted — genuinely, which is rarer than the performance. "We understand each other perfectly. Distrust, stated plainly, is the firmest foundation I know." She rises, and the scarlet settles, and the audience is ending. "Now — tomorrow morning, early. A coffee-house. You will be nobody, and you will see everything. Wear nothing that shines, nothing that strives, nothing the coffee-house will look at twice. It is the hardest thing you will ever wear, darling." She smiles. "Invisibility.")*
> *(At the door Rose turns. "Lavinia." "Darling?" "Thank you." Lavinia considers this — the thanks, the plainness of it — and nods once, as if it were a coin she had decided to accept. The door closes. The scarlet room keeps its counsel.)*
> *(Rose stands a moment on the step, the four offers arranged in her head like cards: corners, tables, benches, Pyke himself. And behind them the withheld file — the old filth, the old invoices, who paid then — sitting behind Lavinia's smile like a drawer not yet opened. Later, darling. Everything with Lavinia is later, except the chocolate, which is now.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Weigh "hatred I have in abundance, and it is free." → Remembered:The transactional register; Lavinia deals in stated terms.
> 2. [choice]Rank the four offers — corners, tables, back tables, Pyke. → Remembered:Her priorities; the week's walks follow the ranking.
> 3. [examine]Study the withheld file — "the old filth, the old invoices." → Remembered:Her understanding of installment trust; she'll earn each disclosure.
> 4. [social]Say "nor am I rich in trust" plainly. → Remembered:The foundation; plain distrust holds better than performed warmth.
> 5. [stance]Receive "invisibility" — with humility or rebellion. → Remembered:Her willingness to be nobody; the S6 ritual is built on it.
> 6. [examine]Test "distrust, stated plainly, is the firmest foundation." → Remembered:Whether she adopts it; it governs the alliance's working shape.
> 7. [micro]Note the rings settling — the gilt's small tell. → Remembered:Her eye for Lavinia's tells; the scarlet composure has seams.
> 8. [choice]Accept tomorrow's dawn summons — or negotiate the hour. → Remembered:Who sets the tempo; dawn accepted is deference, dawn negotiated is partnership.

### L2.S5 · [T] · Rose (reflecting)

*Purpose: the guide, weighed — what Lavinia is; Augusta's warning, remembered.*

> *(Walking home, Rose turns Lavinia over like a coin — and finds the coin has two faces, both genuine. The ally: she knows the gutter the way Augusta knows the ton, from the inside, by long acquaintance, by having been wounded by it young. The scarlet room, the precise handwriting, the decade of studying Pyke's coffee hours — this is not dilettantism. This is a woman who has made hatred her profession, and professions demand respect, even disreputable ones. Especially disreputable ones.)*
> *(The agenda — Pyke broken, not bought — suits Rose's terms so neatly that it frightens her. Neatness, in allies, is usually a sign that one has not yet seen the whole of them. And then Augusta's voice, arriving on schedule: her aim, never her reins. Rose understands, halfway down Milsom Street, what Augusta meant. Not that Lavinia is untrustworthy. That Lavinia is trustworthy about exactly one thing — her hatred of Pyke. That hatred will not waver, will not be bought, will not be distracted by a better offer or a prettier morning. Everything else — the methods, the costs, the collateral, the timing — Lavinia will decide for herself, in the moment, beautifully.)*
> *(Rose's task, then, is not to control her. One does not control Lavinia Crane; one accompanies her, the way one accompanies weather. Keep one's own counsel. Keep one's own map — somewhere Lavinia will never see it, in the campaign book's back pages. Keep one's own exit. The city rearranges itself around her as she walks — not addresses anymore, but territory: corners, tables, benches, the four hands' whole geography. At the door she decides what the household will hear tonight. Some of it. Not the map.)*
> *(The walk home takes her past the linen-draper's, the circulating library, the confectioner's with its trays of ratafia cakes. Ordinary Bath, conducting its ordinary morning — none of it aware that she has just hired a guide to the gutter. She likes the ordinariness: the cakes, the muslins in the window. It steadies her. The gutter can have the coffee-houses and the back benches. This street, this morning, she keeps.)*

### L2.S6 · [C] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the coffee-house morning — the ritual. Direction + occasion brief, ~20 micro-decisions, story morsels between pins, reveal coda. Mid-chapter as preparation: she dresses to be invisible, to walk the gutter unseen. (Turns: 21 — the direction + twenty 5-coin pins)*

> **Occasion brief (published):** "A coffee-house morning, in Lavinia's company, to watch the Lantern travel its four hands. Mourning colors forbidden; gloves expected. **Editorial criterion: discretion.** The brief is the campaign's first field commission: be nobody, see everything."

> **Direction:** the nobody's gown. The occasion is a coffee-house at seven in the morning — clerks, merchants, idlers, the masculine machinery of the city's mornings. The direction is chosen first, and the twenty pins follow the direction. Three readings of the same brief; one will be hers. The ritual is long, and the length is the point: invisibility is not the absence of dressing. It is dressing with the signs reversed.

> **A. The Nobody** — dust and shadow, the unremarkable perfected. Motif: nothing — no motif, no embroidery, no device; the absence is the statement. Thread: undyed linen (the honest) / grey wool (the forgettable) / plain cotton (the cheap, worn well). Colorway: dust merino, ash grey, unbleached muslin. Accessories: none. Not one. The reticule plain, the gloves plain, the hat untrimmed.

> **B. The Respectable Tradesman's Wife** — neatness as camouflage. Motif: small sprigs, printed, not embroidered — the respectable woman's print, bought off the peg. Thread: brown cotton (the thrifty) / dark blue (the sober) / plain stitching (the mended). Colorway: dark blue merino, brown stuff, grey print. Accessories: a plain shawl; serviceable gloves; no jewels; the reticule with the mending in it.

> **C. The Quiet Observer** — the lady, dimmed. Motif: white roses, tiny, at the hem only — the clean name, whispered, not stated. Thread: ivory (the name, kept) / dove (the name, lowered) / silver-grey (the name, veiled). Colorway: dove silk, lavender grey, pearl. Accessories: the smallest pearls; grey kid gloves; a plain hat; no sparkle anywhere.

> **Decomposed steps:** twenty pins after the direction, five coins each — the invisibility's whole argument, decided piece by piece. Re-picks are free, now and after — the atelier keeps every combination. Lavinia's note is explicit — no jewels, not one — and the Gazette will notice the restraint, and approve it.

> 1. [choice]**Direction — the commitment:** A, B, or C — the nobody's reading, chosen first, the pins to follow. → Remembered: the direction; the coffee-house will read her before she sits down.

> 2. [micro]**Pin 1 — the dress's silhouette:** the plain column, no drape to catch the eye; or the fuller skirt's rustle. 5 coins. → Remembered: the line; nobody has no silhouette worth remembering.

> 3. [micro]**Pin 2 — the bodice's cut:** high and closed, the working woman's; or the season's neckline. 5 coins. → Remembered: the throat's covering; the coffee-house looks once and looks away.

> 4. [micro]**Pin 3 — the bodice fabric:** plain merino, honest and dull; cambric, too fine; muslin, too pretty. 5 coins. → Remembered: the cloth's honesty; merino is the nobody's silk.

> *(Story morsel: Letitia comes in with the tea tray and stops in the doorway. "Plain, darling," she says at last, "but not poor — there is a difference, and the coffee-house knows it. Poor is looked through. Plain is looked past. You want the second one.")*

> 5. [micro]**Pin 4 — the bodice color:** dust, ash, unbleached — the palette of not-being-seen. 5 coins. → Remembered: the color's absence; dust is the nobody's scarlet.

> 6. [micro]**Pin 5 — the sleeve style:** plain and narrow, the arm unremarked; or the season's fullness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the arm's plainness; full sleeves are for being looked at.

> 7. [micro]**Pin 6 — the sleeve fabric:** to match the bodice, invisibly; or the contrast's small vanity. 5 coins. → Remembered: the match; contrast is a kind of signature, and nobodies don't sign.

> 8. [micro]**Pin 7 — the neckline:** the small collar, closed; or the open throat's invitation. 5 coins. → Remembered: the collar; closed is the morning's whole argument.

> *(Story morsel: Lavinia's note arrives with the dawn, the handwriting all slant and hurry: "No jewels. Not one. Invisibility is absolute, darling. — L." Rose reads it twice, and puts away the pearl brooch she had almost chosen. Almost does not count. Absolute is absolute.)*

> 9. [micro]**Pin 8 — the skirt's style:** straight and narrow, the walk unimpeded; or the fashionable width. 5 coins. → Remembered: the walk; the nobody must be able to leave quickly.

> 10. [micro]**Pin 9 — the skirt fabric:** the merino, continuing; or the cheaper stuff's honesty. 5 coins. → Remembered: the continuation; one cloth, head to hem, is the uniform of the unseen.

> 11. [micro]**Pin 10 — the skirt color:** dust again, the whole gown one weather; or the darker hem's practicality. 5 coins. → Remembered: the weather; a gown all one dust is a gown the eye slides off.

> 12. [micro]**Pin 11 — the trim:** none — the brave choice, the honest one; or the narrowest binding. 5 coins. → Remembered: the none; trim is handwriting, and this gown is anonymous.

> *(Story morsel: the maid brushes the plain merino with unusual care, as if the cloth's very dullness required coaxing. "Shall I tell the housekeeper you're indisposed, miss?" she asks. "The other houses will ask." "Tell her I am visiting a sick friend," Rose says. "It is not even a lie. I am going to watch a sick trade.")*

> 13. [micro]**Pin 12 — the shoes:** the plainest boots, made for walking; or the slipper's prettiness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the boots; the gutter is walked, not entered.

> 14. [micro]**Pin 13 — the gloves:** plain cotton, the working hand; or the kid's softness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the cotton; kid gloves are for being seen wearing gloves.

> 15. [micro]**Pin 14 — the hair:** scraped back, severe, unremarkable; or the softer style's vanity. 5 coins. → Remembered: the severity; hair is the first thing the eye reads, so give it nothing.

> 16. [micro]**Pin 15 — the hair piece:** the plainest hat, untrimmed — the crown of the invisible gown; or the cap's neatness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hat; untrimmed is the achievement, not the lack.

> *(Story morsel: Élise is not here — no atelier, no fitting, only the maid and the plain merino — and Rose finds she misses the ceremony anyway, the pins and the mirrors and the argument. Then she catches herself: missing the ceremony is the point. The nobody does not get a ceremony. The nobody gets a hat.)*

> 17. [micro]**Pin 16 — the earrings:** none — the ear unadorned; the discipline holds. 5 coins. → Remembered: the none; Lavinia's note was explicit, and explicit is obeyed.

> 18. [micro]**Pin 17 — the pendant:** none — the throat bare under the collar. 5 coins. → Remembered: the bare throat; the brooch stays in the drawer, the lesson stays with her.

> 19. [micro]**Pin 18 — the beauty:** the plain face, no paint, no powder's lie; or the rouge's small rebellion. 5 coins. → Remembered: the plain face; the coffee-house reads paint as a claim, and she claims nothing.

> 20. [micro]**Pin 19 — the reticule:** the plainest, with pennies for coffee and shillings for information; or the better one's vanity. 5 coins. → Remembered: the purse shelf; the nobody goes out equipped, not adorned.

> 21. [micro]**Pin 20 — the finishing:** the shawl, plain wool, for the damp morning; the last layer of nobody. 5 coins. → Remembered: the shawl; the finishing is the weather's, not fashion's.

> *(Rose reads the occasion brief twice — the coffee-house morning, the twenty pins, the hundred coins for the privilege of being nobody. She thinks of every gown she has ever commissioned — every seam a plea, every collar an argument — and understands that this commission is the argument's mirror image: the art identical, the direction reversed. Twenty decisions. Five coins each. The most expensive plain gown she will ever wear.)*

> **Jewels (owned pieces only):** the ritual's jewel step draws on pieces the player already owns — the jewel shelf itself is open at any time, never a ritual step. For this brief, the correct choice is none — Lavinia's note is explicit, and the Gazette will notice (and approve) the restraint.

### L2.S7 · [T] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the mirror — the reveal coda; invisibility, worn; the anti-fashion ritual's quiet triumph.*

> *(The mirror moment: dawn light through the bedroom window, the plain gown — merino the color of dust, no trim, no jewel, hair scraped back under a hat so unremarkable it is almost an achievement. She looks at herself and sees nobody. Not a lady, not an heiress, not a paragraph. It is the strangest triumph of her fashionable life, and she does not entirely trust it. The art is the same; only the direction is reversed. On the dressing table, Lavinia's note: "Invisibility is absolute, darling." She takes off the one forgotten ring — the pearl brooch's small companion — and puts it in the drawer. Absolute. No shine, no strive, no recognition.)*
> *(Letitia, seeing her, puts a hand to her heart. "Like nobody," Rose supplies. "Precisely," says Letitia, faintly, sitting down without being asked. Then Augusta, at the door, in her dressing gown, formidable before breakfast: "Do not flinch. Flinching is recognizable." Rose nods. She does not flinch. She has never once dressed without an audience in mind — the ton, the suitors, the Gazette, even Augusta — and this morning's audience is the gutter, which demands only that she not exist. There is a freedom in it, unexpected and a little vertiginous, like standing too close to a parapet.)*
> *(She goes out into the Bath morning — the street still grey, the lamps still lit — and walks to Lavinia's corner with the plain reticule and the plain boots and the plain, plain hat. To be nobody is to be unjudged, unmentioned, unweighed. She could walk through Bath and leave no paragraph behind. The thought follows her down the street like a small, well-behaved dog. She lets it follow. This morning, she can afford the company.)*
> *(The walk to Lavinia's corner is the first walk of her life in which no one looks at her twice. A crossing-sweeper glances through her. A maid with a basket steps around her without the small assessing pause maids give ladies. It is — she searches for the word — restful. Like taking off a corset no one knew she was wearing. She understands, suddenly, why Lavinia prescribed it: you cannot map a country while the country is busy looking at you.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Study "merino the color of dust." → Remembered:Her vocabulary for invisibility; she'll dress it again.
> 2. [micro]Remove the forgotten ring — "absolute." → Remembered:The removal as ritual; absolute means absolute.
> 3. [stance]Savor "the strangest triumph of her fashionable life." → Remembered:Whether triumph in invisibility tempts her; the gutter's freedom seduces.
> 4. [social]Steady Letitia's hand-to-heart. → Remembered:How she steadies her aunt; nerves are the morning's cost.
> 5. [examine]Take "flinching is recognizable" into the street. → Remembered:Augusta's last counsel; her street discipline.
> 6. [stance]Meet the vertiginous freedom — "unjudged, unmentioned, unweighed." → Remembered:What nobody-hood gives her; the freedom returns as temptation.
> 7. [examine]Reckon with "never once dressed without an audience." → Remembered:Her self-knowledge as performer; it deepens the studio's meaning.

### L2.S8 · [D] · Rose, Lavinia

*Purpose: the coffee-house — arrival; the trade, observed; the men paid to believe.*

> *(The coffee-house is on a corner that smells of roasting and damp wool. Lavinia steers Rose to a corner table — Lavinia herself in grey, subdued, almost plain, which on Lavinia reads as a costume more elaborate than scarlet. Nobody looks at them. The invisibility is working. "This," Lavinia says, low, "is where belief is bought by the cup. Watch. Do not stare. Nobody stares — staring is recognizable.")*
> *(The room: clerks, merchants, idlers, the whole masculine machinery of the city's mornings, arranged around tables like parts of a mill. On every table, the Lantern. A clerk reads it aloud to his table, doing the voices — the Hartwell paragraph in a mincing falsetto that gets the laugh it is designed to get. A merchant folds a copy into his pocket, "for the wife," with the air of a man smuggling lace. And in the corner, a man in a good coat is buying coffee for three younger men, laughing a fraction too loudly at the fraud paragraph — and Rose watches the younger men laugh with him, a fraction too quickly, a fraction too eagerly. The cold clarity arrives all at once: the laughter is purchased. The coffee is the price. The belief is the goods. The younger men are not amused. They are employed.)*
> *("The man in the good coat," Rose says, very quietly, not looking — nobody stares. "Who is he?" "Nobody, and everybody." Lavinia sips her coffee black, without pleasure, like medicine. "He is paid — a few shillings a week, the price of a good dinner — to enjoy the Lantern in public. There is one in every house, darling. The gutter staffs its audience the way the theater staffs its claque.")*
> *(Rose watches the good coat buy another round. The younger men laugh on cue. The clerk does the voices. The merchant's pocket bulges with his wife's copy. It is all so ordinary — the buying, the laughing, the reading aloud — that the ordinariness is the worst of it. Hatred would be easier to answer. This is only business, conducted over coffee, at seven in the morning, and business does not hate her. Business merely bills her.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Read Lavinia "in grey" — restraint as performance. → Remembered:Lavinia is never not performing; the grey becomes data.
> 2. [micro]Practice "do not stare" — the unseeing gaze. → Remembered:Her streetcraft; the discipline serves every walk.
> 3. [examine]Study the clerk "doing the voices." → Remembered:The libel's oral life; it travels by performance.
> 4. [examine]Calibrate to the purchased laughter — "a fraction too loudly." → Remembered:Her detection of paid belief; it returns at the Pump Room.
> 5. [social]Ask "who is he?" very quietly, not looking. → Remembered:How she questions in the field; Lavinia notes technique.
> 6. [examine]Price it — "a few shillings a week." → Remembered:Her ledger of the enemy's costs; the operation's scale.
> 7. [stance]Receive "the belief is the goods." → Remembered:Her emotional discipline; cold clarity is a weapon.
> 8. [choice]Stay longer, or leave. → Remembered:Her appetite for the field; the gutter rewards staying.

### L2.S9 · [T] · Rose (observing)

*Purpose: the geography, audible — her own name, sold for a penny; the hawker's cry.*

> *(They leave by the side door, into the street's roar — and there it is, the sound Lavinia promised, half-dreaded and half-needed. A hawker's cry. It is one thing to read it in London, folded on a breakfast table, the print small and deniable. It is another to hear it cried — her name, her father's name, the Park's name, sold like oysters, a penny a paper, with the gusto of a man selling fresh fish on a good morning. The hawker is a boy, fourteen perhaps, voice too big for his chest, all ferocious cheerful energy. He catches her eye and grins — the salesman's professional grin, practiced on a thousand corners. "'Ere, miss! The 'Artwell Fraud! You'll laugh, miss, it's rich! Best penny you'll spend!")*
> *(She stands there, in her invisible gown, being nobody, and her own ruin is offered to her for a penny — the stack tilted toward her, the masthead up, the boy waiting with the patience of the born salesman. She is tempted. The penny is in her reticule; the buying would be the easiest thing in the world. She does not. Lavinia's hand closes on her arm — not urgently, just firmly, the way one steadies a horse. "Walk on, darling," Lavinia murmurs, without moving her lips. "Walk on, and remember this. Remember the voice. Remember the price." A pause. "Remember that it is a boy." They walk on. Behind them the cry rises again, taken up by another throat further down the street. Rose walks on. She remembers.)*
> *(The gutter does not hate her. That is the morning's real intelligence, and it arrives without fanfare: the street is merely busy — busy the way a mill is busy, the wheels turning, the grain fed in, no malice in the mechanism. The boy is not her enemy; he is a boy with a stack of papers and a grin, and he would cry any name for a penny with exactly the same gusto. Busyness can be outlasted. Hatred cannot. She files it with the four hands and the purchased laughter — the campaign's whole doctrine, learned before breakfast, at the cost of one unspent penny.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [stance]Hear her name cried — dread or need. → Remembered:Honesty about needing to hear it; inoculates or wounds.
> 2. [micro]The boy's professional grin — meet it or look away. → Remembered:What the gutter's youngest reads in her face.
> 3. [examine]Weigh "remember that it is a boy." → Remembered:Refusing to hate hawkers; the war aims at payers.
> 4. [choice]The temptation to buy — why she doesn't. → Remembered:The refusal defines her relation to the trade.
> 5. [examine]"The gutter does not hate her; it is merely busy." → Remembered:The reframe; outlasting becomes doctrine.
> 6. [stance]Take "walk on, and remember this." → Remembered:How she takes field commands; authority negotiated.
> 7. [social]Lavinia's hand — "not urgently, just firmly." → Remembered:The physical trust; deepens or checks.
> 8. [micro]The threefold memory — voice, price, boy. → Remembered:Evidence for the payer's finding.

### L2.S10 · [D] · Rose, Lavinia

*Purpose: the four hands — the route, mapped aloud; the night's journey of a libel.*

> *(They walk now, away from the cry, toward the next corner where another cry rises — the street full of them, like birds at dawn, each with the same song. "Four hands, one night," Lavinia says, falling into step. "The press — Pyke's chapel sets the type by midnight, by lamplight, the whole damp business done while honest people sleep. The mail coach — down the Bath road overnight, the bundles in by dawn, tossed at the inn yard with the coal. The hawkers — boys like that one, paid by the stack, extra for the loud corners, the loudest corners to the loudest boys. The coffee-houses — free copies on the tables by seven, because a free paper is read twice and talked about three times. The Pump Room. By dinner, every dining room on the Crescent has mentioned it. By the next morning, it is known." She glances sideways. "Not the same as believed, darling. But in the ton, it amounts to the same thing.")*
> *("And the extra pay?" Rose asks. "The man in the good coat. The laughter, purchased. Who pays him? Who pays for the sky over this gutter?" "Ah." Lavinia stops walking — and for the first time the glitter dims, banked like a fire covered for the night. "That is the question. Not Pyke — Pyke's purse is shallow; a penny paper cannot afford a dozen men in good coats buying coffee all over Bath. Someone pays him. Someone with a purse deeper than Fleet Street, and a patience — or an impatience — greater than Pyke's.")*
> *(They stand on the corner while the street moves around them. "The map has a center now, darling," Lavinia says quietly. "Blank. Occupied by a question." Rose thinks of the campaign book, waiting at home — the map she has not yet drawn, the center she has not yet left blank. "We are going to find out where the money comes from," Lavinia says. It is not a question. Rose nods. It is the first thing they have fully agreed on, and the agreement feels like ground underfoot — narrow ground, but ground.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]The night's journey — press to club, each hand's hour. → Remembered:Her timetable; she'll use it to intercept.
> 2. [choice]"Who pays for the sky over this gutter?" → Remembered:She asked the real question first; respected.
> 3. [examine]Prove "Pyke's purse is shallow." → Remembered:Arithmetic demanding a deeper payer; the mystery's foundation.
> 4. [stance]"Patience — or impatience — greater than Pyke's." → Remembered:Her profile of the payer; patience plans, impatience spends.
> 5. [micro]The map "gains a center — blank." → Remembered:Tolerance for the unanswered; the blank drives Ch3.
> 6. [examine]"Known — in the ton, amounts to believed." → Remembered:The ton's epistemology; shapes the counter-campaign.
> 7. [social]Nod — seal the compact. → Remembered:The deepening; the center found together, or not.
> 8. [choice]Claim the hunt — "we are going to find out." → Remembered:Who leads; leadership determines risks.

### L2.S11 · plot beat · Rose

*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — THE GUTTER, WALKED OR SENT.*

> *(The four hands are mapped. The blank center waits. And Rose understands, standing on the corner with Lavinia, that the next decision is not about Pyke or the payer or the paper — it is about her own feet. The gutter can be walked by agents — men paid to drink coffee and listen, women paid to sit on the back benches and report. Respectable. Safe. Distant. It can be walked by Lavinia — the guide knows every corner, every cry, every table, and would love nothing better than to conduct the whole campaign herself, with Rose as the audience. Or it can be walked by Rose. In the invisible gown. At seven in the morning, with the clerks and the coffee and the purchased laughter, being nobody, seeing everything.)*
> *(Augusta's counsel, weighed: the ton will forgive much, but it does not forgive being seen to fight dirty — and the gutter is dirty by definition, damp and loud and smelling of roasting coffee. Lavinia's counsel, weighed: a guide can only guide; at some point the general walks her own ground. And her own counsel, which is the newest and the least certain: the map in the campaign book is still blank, and a map drawn from reports is a map drawn by other hands. She wants the geography under her own boots. She wants to hear the cry herself, and price the laughter herself, and look the boy in the eye herself.)*
> *(Three roads. Walk it herself: the invisible gown, the coffee-houses, the Pump Room's back benches, the gutter under her own boots — dangerous, improper, irreplaceable. Send agents: respectable, safe, the reports arriving folded and deniable — and every report a translation, and every translation a loss. Let Lavinia guide: the scarlet expert, the decade of hatred, the whole campaign conducted in expert company — and the map, at the end, Lavinia's map, not hers. The street is busy. The cry rises again down the road. The decision, like the geography, is hers to walk.)*
*★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — the gutter, walked or sent:*
- **Walk it herself** — *Agency+; the field general.* "I will walk it. In the invisible gown — coffee-houses, corners, Pump Room. Myself." *(Lavinia: "Brave. Or reckless. We shall see which.")*
- **Send agents** — *Caution+; the distant general.* Respectable, deniable; paid listeners at the tables, paid eyes on the corners. *(Augusta approves; Lavinia is disappointed, and says so.)*
- **Let Lavinia guide** — *Alliance+; the conducted tour.* Lavinia's decade of hatred, Lavinia's corners, Lavinia's map. *(The knowledge is expert; the map, at the end, is hers.)*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [choice]Decide who walks — self, agents, Lavinia. → Remembered:Her style of generalship; the whole campaign's posture set here.
> 2. [stance]Take "the gutter is dirty by definition." → Remembered:How she regards the field; it sets her tolerance.
> 3. [examine]Price "every report a translation, and every translation a loss." → Remembered:Her epistemology; firsthand knowledge has a price she's willing to pay.
> 4. [social]Weigh Lavinia's eagerness to conduct. → Remembered:Whether she lets the guide lead; the reins question, again.
> 5. [examine]Test "a map drawn from reports is a map drawn by other hands." → Remembered:Her authorship of the campaign book; it stays hers.
> 6. [stance]Answer "brave. Or reckless." → Remembered:Her answer to the charge; it defines her courage's register.
> 7. [choice]Deliver the decision — to Lavinia's face, or in writing. → Remembered:The delivery's form; Lavinia reads ceremony like cards.
> *(The game remembers the choice — and the means: the field general's boots, the distant general's agents, the conducted tour. The gutter will be walked either way — the question was always by whom.)*

### L2.S12 · [T] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the second coffee-house — alone; the gutter's tiredness; what repetition costs.*

> *(The next morning she goes alone. The invisibility holds — the plain merino, the untrimmed hat, the nobody's whole kit — and the coffee-house admits her without a glance. She takes a corner table, orders coffee, and watches the room do what it did yesterday: the clerk doing the voices, the merchant's pocket bulging, the Lantern folded on every table like a shared tablecloth. But something is different. Or perhaps she is. Yesterday the libel was a geography — four hands, one night, a system to be mapped. Today it is only tired.)*
> *(The tiredness is the gutter's, not hers. The same paragraph, read aloud by the same clerk, in the same mincing falsetto, to the same laugh — and the laugh is thinner today, the way a joke is thinner the third time. She watches a man read the Hartwell paragraph, fold the paper, and reach for another — the whole thing consumed in the time it takes coffee to cool. Consumed. That is the word that arrives, uninvited and exact. The libel is not believed in this room. It is consumed — like the coffee, like the toast, like the morning itself. Weaker than belief, and stronger: it does not need to be true to be repeated. It only needs to be there.)*
> *(No letters to the Lantern. No answers in the Gazette. She will let the tune play itself out, morning by morning, until it is only background — if she does not keep conducting it by answering it. She has conducted it enough. The baton, this morning, stays in the drawer.)*
> *(She drinks her coffee. It is indifferent coffee. She leaves a penny tip she cannot quite afford — the nobody's purse is lean, and the penny matters — and walks out into the Bath morning with the tiredness following her like a damp hem. Repetition is the gutter's currency. She understands that now, in her bones, the way one understands weather. The same tune, played every morning, until the tune is only background. The libel is not a wound that deepens. It is a noise that fades — if she lets it fade, if she does not keep conducting it by answering it. She walks on. The penny is spent. The lesson is kept.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [micro]Go alone — the invisibility unescorted. → Remembered:Her independence in the field; Lavinia notes the solo walk.
> 2. [examine]Name it "tired" — the libel's second-day register. → Remembered:Her ear for the libel's fatigue; it becomes strategy.
> 3. [examine]Take "consumed, not believed." → Remembered:The economics; outrage's opposite, and stronger.
> 4. [stance]Watch "the laugh thinner" — the joke's third telling. → Remembered:Her patience with repetition; the gutter's limit observed.
> 5. [micro]The penny tip "she cannot quite afford." → Remembered:Her honesty about the nobody's purse; the price of the field.
> 6. [examine]Weigh "it does not need to be true to be repeated." → Remembered:The mechanism; it shapes the whole counter-campaign.
> 7. [stance]Choose not to conduct it — "if she does not keep conducting it by answering it." → Remembered:Her restraint; the libel starved of response.
> 8. [micro]Walk out — the damp hem of tiredness. → Remembered:The walk; the gutter's weather, walked through.

### L2.S13 · [D] · Rose, Augusta

*Purpose: the report — the gutter, described to the general; what to do about Lavinia.*

> *(She reports at breakfast, the way Augusta likes it: fully, in order, no embroidery. The coffee-houses. The purchased laughter — the man in the good coat, the few shillings a week, the belief bought by the cup. The hawker's boy, fourteen, the cry, the penny. The four hands, one night. Lavinia's agenda — named, priced, the terms stated. Augusta listens the way she does everything: completely, and without moving a muscle in her face.)*
> *("And the guide?" Augusta asks, when the report is done. "Lavinia Crane is — " Rose hesitates, and Augusta waits, and the waiting does the work. "Lavinia Crane is exactly what she appears to be, Grandmama. A woman with a ten-year hatred and a scarlet room and a map of the gutter drawn from the inside. Her aim is clean. Her methods — " "Her methods are hers." Augusta finishes it. "Her aim, never her reins, child. You have it right. Hold it.")*
> *("There is one thing more." Rose sets down her cup. "The most dangerous sentence in politics — she said it, or I did, I no longer remember which. 'The gutter is not fought from the gutter.' Augusta — " She looks at her grandmother. "Do I let her take me anywhere I would not go alone?" Augusta considers this — the question, the honesty of it — and answers with the general's economy. "No. You go where the map requires. You take her knowledge, her corners, her introductions. And you do not let her take you anywhere you would not go alone. Not once. Not for the alliance, not for the hatred, not for the excellent chocolate.")*
> *(Letitia, at the sideboard, makes a small sound — half laugh, half protest. Augusta does not smile. Generals do not smile when giving orders that matter. The orders are given. The map requires the gutter. Rose finishes her breakfast.)*
> *(Afterward, Augusta refills her own cup — the interview over, the orders given. "The boy," she says. "Fourteen. Remember that it is a boy. Wars are not won by hating boys." Rose nods. She has already filed the boy under not-enemy. The campaign book waits upstairs, and the map, and the blank center. The general has spoken. The field general goes to draw.)*

### L2.S14 · [T] · Rose, Lavinia (observed)

*Purpose: the Pump Room's back benches — conversation as the final press; the map's last hand.*

> *(The Pump Room at noon is a different animal from the coffee-house at seven. The coffee-house is masculine, mercantile, loud. The Pump Room is the ton at its most aqueous — the waters drunk, the strolls taken, the whole of Bath society circulating in a slow, glittering current. And at the back, on the benches, the current slows. This is where the morning's reading becomes the afternoon's conversation. Lavinia steers Rose to a bench with the air of a woman showing a visitor the engine room.)*
> *("Have you seen." The phrase moves along the benches like a relay — never shouted, never cried, only murmured, passed from hand to hand like a fan. "Have you seen the Lantern?" "Have you seen what it says?" Never "do you believe." Belief is not required. Only the seeing. Only the passing-on. Rose sits very still — the invisible gown, the untrimmed hat, nobody at all — and watches her own name travel the benches the way a leaf travels water: carried, turned, carried again, never sinking, never arriving anywhere in particular.)*
> *(A woman in puce — three chins, a formidable cameo — leans to her companion and says, distinctly, "The Hartwell girl. Have you seen?" The companion has seen. They have all seen. The seeing is the whole of it. And Rose understands, with the cold clarity that is becoming the week's habit: this is the final hand. Not the press, not the coach, not the hawker, not the coffee-house table. This. The back benches. Conversation. The only press that matters — because conversation, unlike print, cannot be answered, cannot be sued, cannot be starved. It can only be outlasted, or replaced.)*
> *(She takes out the campaign book — no, not the book, the small memorandum card she carries for the field — and writes, in the tiny hand: "Back benches. 'Have you seen.' Never 'do you believe.' The final hand." Lavinia watches her write, and smiles — the professional's smile, the guide's approval. "The map's last hand, darling," Lavinia murmurs. "You have walked the whole geography now. Press to Pump Room. Four hands, one night, and a morning's conversation. It is all one road.")*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Contrast the Pump Room "aqueous" with the coffee-house. → Remembered:Her ear for the ton's registers; each room has its weather.
> 2. [examine]Track "have you seen" along the benches. → Remembered:The phrase; it becomes the campaign's key intelligence.
> 3. [stance]Sit "very still" — nobody at all. → Remembered:Her discipline of stillness; the back benches reward it.
> 4. [examine]Watch her name "travel like a leaf on water." → Remembered:Her understanding of conversation's physics; it cannot be answered, only outlasted.
> 5. [social]Hear "the Hartwell girl" — distinctly. → Remembered:Her composure under direct mention; the field tests it.
> 6. [examine]Take "the final hand." → Remembered:The map's completion; press to Pump Room, one road.
> 7. [micro]Write the memorandum — the tiny hand. → Remembered:Her field notes; the campaign book grows by inches.
> 8. [social]Receive Lavinia's "professional's smile." → Remembered:The guide's approval; earned, not given.

### L2.S15 · [F] · Rose (alone)

*Purpose: the hat — the fashion micro-choice; what the Pump Room takes, what it gives back.*

> *(The Pump Room has a custom, unwritten and absolute: a lady does not sit on the back benches in a morning hat and leave with it unremarked. Or rather — the custom is subtler than that. The Pump Room notices everything, remarks on nothing, and remembers forever. Rose's hat — plain, untrimmed, the crown of the invisible gown — has done its work. It has made her nobody. But the Pump Room, she is learning, has a price for its benches, and the price is not always paid in coin.)*
> *(A woman in puce — the three chins, the formidable cameo — has been looking at the hat. Not at Rose. At the hat. The look is professional, the look of a woman who has priced a thousand hats and found most of them wanting. "My dear," she says, when the waters are drunk and the strolling begins, "that hat. It is — " She pauses. The pause is the whole sentence. "It is plain," Rose supplies, helpfully, which is not what the woman meant at all. "Plain," the woman repeats, and there is something in her voice — not unkindness, exactly, but the ton's particular arithmetic, the sum every woman does in her head when she sees another woman's hat.)*
> *(She has been priced by hats before — every woman in Bath has. The bonnet question is the ton's oldest intelligence test: too fine, and you are striving; too plain, and you are either poor or making a point, and the Pump Room can never quite decide which is worse. Her hat — plain merino, untrimmed, the nobody's crown — has passed the test by refusing to take it. The woman in puce is still looking. The looking is the price of the benches. Rose lets her look.)*
> *(Rose understands the choice, suddenly and completely. The hat can stay — plain, untrimmed, the nobody's crown, the Pump Room's benches paid for in invisibility. The hat can be given — to the woman in puce, a courtesy, a small coin of the ton's own currency, the hat becoming a story she tells at dinner. Or the hat can be retired — burned, metaphorically, the invisible gown's whole kit struck, the nobody dismissed with thanks. Three roads. The Pump Room watches. The Pump Room always watches, remarks on nothing, remembers forever.)*
*★ FASHION CHOICE — the hat:*
- **Keep it** — *The nobody's crown, kept.* The plain hat stays — the invisible gown's whole kit intact, the Pump Room's benches still open to her. *(The woman in puce is disappointed; the gutter remains walkable.)*
- **Give it** — *A courtesy, paid.* The hat goes to the woman in puce — a small coin of the ton's currency, the hat becoming a dinner story. *(The woman is delighted; the nobody loses her crown.)*
- **Retire it** — *The kit, struck.* The invisible gown's whole kit retired — the nobody dismissed with thanks, the field work done another way. *(Lavinia is amused: "Retired, darling? Already?")*
> **Turns (7):**
> 1. [examine]Read the woman in puce — "the ton's particular arithmetic." → Remembered:Her literacy in the ton's sums; every hat is priced.
> 2. [stance]Hear the pause — "it is — " → Remembered:Her composure under the unfinished sentence; the Pump Room tests it.
> 3. [social]Supply "it is plain" helpfully. → Remembered:Her deflection; plainness stated plainly disarms.
> 4. [choice]Weigh the three roads — keep, give, retire. → Remembered:Her pricing of the nobody's kit; the field work's future hangs on it.
> 5. [examine]Take "the Pump Room has a price for its benches." → Remembered:Her understanding of the ton's tolls; nothing is free, not even invisibility.
> 6. [micro]Touch the hat — the untrimmed crown. → Remembered:Her affection for the kit; the nobody was hard-won.
> 7. [choice]Decide — and live with the Pump Room's memory. → Remembered:The decision; the Pump Room remarks on nothing and remembers forever.
*Animation: Shared.*

### L2.S16 · [D] · Rose, Letitia

*Purpose: the comic horror — the aunt's reaction to the gutter; the invisible gown at the Upper Rooms.*

> *("You went where?" Letitia sets down her embroidery — a third dog, or possibly a cat — and stares. Rose has reported the week: the coffee-houses, the back benches, the hawker's boy, the invisible gown. Letitia listens with the expression of a woman hearing that her niece has taken up bare-knuckle boxing.)*
> *("A coffee-house, Aunt. At seven in the morning. In the invisible gown." "A coffee-house." Letitia repeats it the way one repeats a diagnosis. "Where men drink coffee. And read newspapers. Aloud." "They do the voices, Aunt." "The voices." Letitia closes her eyes. "Your grandmother knows of this?" "My grandmother," Rose says carefully, "knows of everything. It is her principal hobby.")*
> *("And the Pump Room's back benches. In that hat." Letitia gestures at the hat, which is sitting on the chair like an uninvited guest. "Darling, I have worn hats to the Pump Room for thirty years, and I have never — " She stops. "I cannot even finish the sentence. The back benches." She picks up the embroidery and puts it down again. "Well. This is commerce, darling. That is what your grandmother would say. This is commerce, and commerce has its customs, and one of them is apparently sitting on back benches in untrimmed hats listening to men do voices.")*
> *(Letitia picks up the embroidery — the dog, or the cat — and stares at it as if it might explain. It does not explain. Nothing this week explains. That, Rose thinks, is rather the point: the gutter is not a riddle to be solved over tea. It is a trade to be learned on foot.)*
> *(Rose laughs — she cannot help it — and Letitia, after a moment, laughs too, a little wildly, the way one laughs at a funeral when the vicar drops the prayer book. "You are," Letitia says, recovering, wiping her eyes, "the very image of your grandmother at your age, except that she would never have been caught dead in that hat, and I mean that as the highest compliment." She picks up the embroidery — the dog, or the cat — and resumes. "The invisible gown at the Upper Rooms, next, I suppose. You will be the first woman in Bath to attend a ball as a rumor.")*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [social]Report the week — fully, to the aunt. → Remembered:Her honesty with Letitia; the household's trust is maintained by it.
> 2. [examine]Weather "bare-knuckle boxing" — the aunt's horror. → Remembered:Her composure under familial alarm; the field work continues anyway.
> 3. [social]Deliver "they do the voices, Aunt." → Remembered:Her deadpan; the comedy is a weapon, too.
> 4. [examine]Take "this is commerce, darling." → Remembered:Letitia's accidental wisdom; commerce has customs, and Rose is learning them.
> 5. [stance]Laugh — "she cannot help it." → Remembered:Her laughter; the war's pressure needs the release.
> 6. [social]Receive "the very image of your grandmother." → Remembered:The comparison; it lands as the highest compliment.
> 7. [examine]Note the embroidery — "a third dog, or possibly a cat." → Remembered:The scene's comic detail; the household's texture.
> 8. [micro]Picture "the first woman in Bath to attend a ball as a rumor." → Remembered:The joke; it becomes the season's private motto.
