### L3.S6 · [C] · Rose (alone) — RITUAL: DRESSING FOR THE WAREHOUSE MORNING
*Purpose: the warehouse morning — the ritual. (Turns: 21 — the direction + twenty 5-coin pins)*

> *(The morning of the warehouse visit begins before dawn, in the London bedroom with the shutters still closed. Rose sits at the glass while the maid lays out the choices, and Augusta's counsel from last night sits with her like a second maid: men with ledgers respect two things, child — exactness, and not wasting their time. The gown must argue before she opens her mouth. It must say: I count correctly, I keep appointments, I am not here to be decorative. It must say it in serge.)*

> *(Augusta's parting counsel, delivered at the bedroom door: "Creech will read the gown before you speak, child. See that it reads true." The door closes. Rose turns back to the glass.)*

> **Occasion brief (published):** "A warehouse morning — Mr. Creech, the ledgers, the trade. The town will hear about this visit before noon; the dressing must survive the retelling. This is not a ball and not a rout — it is business, conducted in a room full of men who sell paper by the ton. **Editorial criterion: credibility.**"

> **Direction:** the warehouse's gown. The occasion is business beyond fashion — the merchant's desk, the warehousemen's stares, the trade's own ground. The direction is chosen first, and the twenty pins follow the direction. Three readings of the same brief; one will be hers. The ritual is long — the gown's whole argument, decided piece by piece — and the length is the point: Creech will read her before she speaks, and the reading must be exact.

> **A. The Woman of Business** — dark serge, the merchant's own cloth. Motif: the ledger's ruling — fine pinstripes, the column's discipline worked into the weave, arithmetic you can wear. Thread: steel-grey silk (the column) / dark silver (the figure) / black silk (the total). Colorway: charcoal serge, iron grey, black. Accessories: the plain pendant, nothing else; dark gloves; sensible half-boots; no fan — a woman of business carries papers, not fans.

> **B. The Hartwell** — the name, worn as armor. Motif: white roses, embroidered small and severe at the hem — the clean name, stated once and not repeated, the flower's language spoken quietly. Thread: ivory whitework (the name, lit) / silver (the name, stated) / pale gold (the name, gilded). Colorway: dove grey silk, white, silver. Accessories: the gold locket, the grandmother's; grey kid gloves; a sash at the waist; silk slippers; the ivory fan, closed.

> **C. The Quiet Authority** — severity without coldness. Motif: no motif — the plain cloth, the perfect cut, the argument made by absence; the most expensive statement in the room is the one that declines to make one. Thread: none — the cloth speaks unembroidered. Colorway: deep navy merino, black, white linen at the throat. Accessories: the silver brooch, the season's first; white kid gloves; no necklace; half-boots; no fan.

> **Decomposed steps:** twenty pins, five coins each — the gown's whole argument, decided piece by piece. Re-picks are free, now and after — the atelier keeps every combination, and the choosing can be revisited without cost. No Crowns touch this ritual; the dressing is the player's own, bought with play.

> 1. [choice]**Direction — the commitment:** A, B, or C — the warehouse's reading, chosen first, the pins to follow. → Remembered: the direction; Creech will read the gown before she speaks.

> 2. [micro]**Pin 1 — the bodice's cut:** high and plain, the business line; or the season's lower ease. 5 coins. → Remembered: the neckline's argument; the warehouse reads modesty as seriousness.

> 3. [micro]**Pin 2 — the bodice fabric:** merino, cambric, or fine serge — the maid's three bolts. 5 coins. → Remembered: the cloth's weight; serge is the merchant's own tongue.

> *(Story morsel: the maid holds up the serge, doubtful. "It is very* plain*, miss." "It is very* certain*," Rose corrects her, and the maid — who has dressed three Hartwell women — nods slowly, filing the correction away with the pins.)*

> 4. [micro]**Pin 3 — the bodice color:** charcoal, iron grey, or black. 5 coins. → Remembered: the color's gravity; dark as ink, plain as a column.

> 5. [micro]**Pin 4 — the sleeve style:** long and plain, the working woman; or the season's fullness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the arm's statement; plain sleeves say the work, not the ornament.

> 6. [micro]**Pin 5 — the sleeve fabric and color:** to match the bodice, always — a divided sleeve argues with itself. 5 coins. → Remembered: the sleeve's unity; the gown must agree with itself.

> 7. [micro]**Pin 6 — the neckline:** a linen collar, a plain band, bare. 5 coins. → Remembered: the throat's framing; the collar is the business line made cloth.

> *(Story morsel: Lavinia's note, kept from yesterday — "a lady who means business" — sits propped against the pincushion while Rose dresses. It is the only ornament on the table, and somehow the most expensive one there.)*

> 8. [micro]**Pin 7 — the skirt's fullness:** the walking sweep, narrowed for warehouse aisles; or the full morning width. 5 coins. → Remembered: the skirt's practicality; she will walk between stacked reams.

> 9. [micro]**Pin 8 — the skirt fabric:** serge to match, or the lighter merino. 5 coins. → Remembered: the skirt's weight; the whole gown must speak one language.

> 10. [micro]**Pin 9 — the skirt color:** charcoal throughout, or the grey below. 5 coins. → Remembered: the color's continuity; a broken color is a broken argument.

> 11. [micro]**Pin 10 — the trim:** none, a narrow braid, a single row of stitching. 5 coins. → Remembered: the trim's restraint; in a warehouse, ornament is noise.

> *(Story morsel: Augusta looks in, on her way down to breakfast, and surveys the pins. "Men with ledgers respect two things, child: exactness, and not wasting their time." She nods at the serge. "That is exact. Be quick about the rest." The door closes. Rose is quick about the rest.)*

> 12. [micro]**Pin 11 — the shoes:** the sensible half-boots; or the silk slippers, for after. 5 coins. → Remembered: the foot's sense; warehouse floors are stone, and stone is honest.

> 13. [micro]**Pin 12 — the gloves:** dark kid, wrist length — the handshake's glove. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hand's business; she will shake a merchant's hand today.

> 14. [micro]**Pin 13 — the hair:** dressed plain and high, the working knot; or the season's softness. 5 coins. → Remembered: the head's composure; the knot is the gown's roof.

> 15. [micro]**Pin 14 — the hair piece:** none — severity's whole point; or the plain comb. 5 coins. → Remembered: the hair's honesty; nothing up there but hair.

> *(Story morsel: Letitia appears in the doorway in her wrapper, clutching her tea. "Severe, darling — but not* sad*." She studies the effect. "There is a difference, and Creech will know it." She is right, and they both know she is right, which is the pleasantest kind of morning.)*

> 16. [micro]**Pin 15 — the earrings:** none; or the small studs. 5 coins. → Remembered: the ear's silence; the warehouse has enough noise.

> 17. [micro]**Pin 16 — the pendant:** the plain one, at most — the Gazette judges ornament in a warehouse as unseriousness. 5 coins. → Remembered: her restraint; one plain pendant is the whole of the jewelry.

> 18. [micro]**Pin 17 — the beauty:** the plain face, the brushed hair, no paint — credibility has no cosmetics. 5 coins. → Remembered: the face's plainness; she will be believed, not admired.

> 19. [micro]**Pin 18 — the finishing accessory:** the campaign book, carried openly — the working woman's reticule. 5 coins. → Remembered: the book at her side; it is the gown's final argument.

> 20. [micro]**Pin 19 — the wrap:** the plain pelisse for the carriage; or none, the cold be damned. 5 coins. → Remembered: the wrap's practicality; October mornings are honest too.

> 21. [micro]**Pin 20 — the entrance:** the punctual arrival, ten minutes early — credibility keeps time. 5 coins. → Remembered: the arrival's timing; early is the whole of the argument.

> *(Reveal coda: the gown, complete — the twenty pins, the direction, the whole argument made cloth. Rose stands before the glass, and the woman in the serge looks back: plain, exact, unignorable. "Very certain," the maid says quietly, from the doorway. The reveal is staged — the bedroom's grey morning light, the serge's hush — and the gown is hers, pin by pin, choice by choice. She takes up the campaign book and goes down to breakfast, ten minutes early.)*

> **Jewels (owned pieces only):** the jewel shelf is open for browsing — the pieces she owns, each with its history: the gold locket, the grandmother's; the clean name's pearls; the silver brooch, the season's first. But no jewel is a ritual step; the pins decide the gown, and the jewels only attend it. Severe and minimal — a plain pendant at most; the Gazette judges ornament in a warehouse as unseriousness. Re-picks are free, and post-scene re-styling is always free — the atelier keeps every combination.

### L3.S7 · [D] · Rose, Creech
*Purpose: the courting of trade — whatever the decision, the working terms; the quantities, remembered.*
> *(The public ledger lies open between them, and the figures sit there like stones in a field — too many, too neat, too damning to be accidental. Rose looks at Creech across the desk: the broad man in the brown coat, the ink under his fingernails, the thirty years of trade in the set of his shoulders. She has one card left to play, and it is not a threat. Augusta said: court him. So she courts him — the way one courts anyone worth having: by asking for the smallest true thing, and meaning it.)*
> *("Mr. Creech," she says. "I am fighting a paper. Not with paper." She lets that sit. "What I need from you is not courage. It is* arithmetic*.")*
> *("Arithmetic," Creech repeats — the word unfamiliar in his mouth, like a coin from a country he has not visited.)*
> *("The quantities. The dates. The coin. Nothing more. I will not ask you to testify. I will not ask you to choose." She meets his eyes. "I ask only that when I come back — and I* will *come back — the ledger is where I left it. That is the whole of the courting, Mr. Creech: arithmetic, kept honest.")*
> *(Creech looks at her for a long moment — the look of a man revising his opinion of ladies, of warnings, of wars. Then he does something extraordinary: he rises, crosses to the far shelf, and takes down a second ledger — smaller, bound in darker leather, the clasp worn bright — and opens it to a page marked with a ribbon, the ribbon worn soft with handling.)*
> *("The private book, miss." He says it quietly — the quiet of a man crossing a line he has avoided for eight weeks. "The coin's* weight*. Counted by me, every time — every sovereign, every visit. The clerk's description — 'a thin man, miss, with a scarred knuckle. I notice hands; it is my trade.' You did not ask me to choose, Miss Hartwell." He looks at the ribbon, soft with handling. "But I have been selling paper to a libel for two months, and I am* tired *of it.")*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]State the ask — "not courage; arithmetic." → Remembered:Her ask; arithmetic, kept honest, is the courting.
> 2. [social]Promise the ledger's place — "where I left it." → Remembered:Her promise; the working terms.
> 3. [micro]Watch him take down the private book. → Remembered:The moment he chose; the ribbon's wear measures tiredness.
> 4. [examine]Take the coin's weight — every sovereign. → Remembered:The weights; the campaign's first real weapon.
> 5. [examine]Keep "a thin man, with a scarred knuckle." → Remembered:The scarred knuckle; the trail's next link is a hand.
> 6. [stance]Hear "tired of a customer who pays too well." → Remembered:Creech's tiredness; trade has its own honor.
> 7. [examine]Receive "not a witness, but arithmetic." → Remembered:What she was given; arithmetic is the weapon.
> 8. [social]Thank him — in trade's own coin. → Remembered:Her thanks; the alliance's manners matter.

### L3.S8 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the private book — the coin's weight, counted; the evidence, held.*
> *(She copies the figures into the campaign book at Creech's desk — the dates, the weights, the clerk's description, the tripling dated to the week the Hartwell numbers began. Her handwriting, usually so careful, goes small and tight with the effort of exactness. But this is different from the Bath maps: this is evidence* with a pulse *— the living arithmetic of a lie being manufactured, counted out in gold on a paper merchant's desk, week after week, edges up, in tens. She can feel it under her pen like a heartbeat.)*
> *(Creech walks her to the warehouse door himself — past the stacked reams, past the warehousemen gone quiet at their work, out into the October light. It is a courtesy he extends, she understands, to very few: to customers who pay in gold and mean well, which is to say, to almost no one. At the door he stops. "Miss Hartwell," he says. "Whatever you do with it — do it in the open. It is the only thing the gutter cannot* arrange*. The gutter arranges shadows, miss. It cannot arrange daylight.")*
> *(She thinks of Letitia: "disarrangement." She thinks of Augusta: "win in the open." She thinks of the terms, set last night over cold eggs in the London dining room: we buy no judge. Three voices, one sentence, in three different accents. The paper is behind her — the reams, the ledgers, the gold. The press is ahead. And between them, the hand with the scarred knuckle, carrying both. She walks out into the street with the campaign book under her arm, and the October air tastes of coal smoke and something else — something like the beginning of the end of something.)*
> *(The copying takes the better part of an hour. Her pen scratches away. Creech pretends to check invoices and does not check them at all, but stands guard at the desk like a mastiff: the broad back, the brown coat, the occasional clearing of the throat when a warehouseman comes too near. When she finishes, her fingers are ink-stained to the second knuckle, and Creech looks at the stains with something like approval. "Trade's own mark, miss," he says. "Washes off by Thursday. The figures don't.")*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [micro]Copy the figures — dates, weights, tripling; the transcription. → Remembered:Her copy; the campaign book holds the pulse.
> 2. [examine]Feel "evidence with a pulse" — the living arithmetic. → Remembered:What pulsed evidence does; it's the first of its kind.
> 3. [social]Receive the door courtesy — "to very few"; the honor. → Remembered:Creech's honor; she's been counted among the few.
> 4. [examine]Keep "do it in the open" — the merchant's counsel. → Remembered:The daylight doctrine; it governs the campaign's honor.
> 5. [stance]Hear Letitia and Augusta in it — "disarrangement," "win in the open." → Remembered:The household's doctrines, confirmed; the chorus harmonizes.
> 6. [examine]Remember the terms — "we buy no judge," over cold eggs in London. → Remembered:The London terms; they're the campaign's constitution.
> 7. [micro]Walk out — paper behind, press ahead, the hand between. → Remembered:The morning's image; it becomes the chapter's emblem.
> 8. [stance]Carry the pulse — the evidence's weight; the trust. → Remembered:What she carries; pulsed evidence is a trust.

### L3.S9 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the firewall — Creech's name, protected; the trail's cost, contained.*
> *(The figures are in the campaign book now — the quantities, the dates, the weight of the coin, the scarred knuckle, all in her small tight hand. But a name is missing from the page, and the missing is deliberate. Creech. She writes it nowhere — not in the book, not in a letter, not in the margin of anything Pyke's people might ever see. What she learned in the warehouse was given by a man who crossed a line he had avoided for eight weeks; the least she owes him is the line's invisibility. A name, once written, can be found. An unwritten name can only be guessed at, and guessing is slower than reading.)*
> *(She tells Augusta at tea: his name is not spoken outside this house. Augusta nods once — never spend another person's safety; it is the first rule of every war she has ever run. She tells Quill in the morning room: the source is protected, or there is no source. Quill frowns — a protected witness is a useless witness, and his frown says so plainly — and then, slowly, nods too. A used witness is a ruined man, and ruined men make poor allies, and Quill — for all his surgeon's manner — understands ruin the way other men understand weather.)*
> *(The firewall is built. Whatever the trail costs from here — and trails, she is learning, always send a bill — Creech does not pay it. Two streets away the warehouse ticks on, the paper settling in the shafts of light, the ledgers sitting where she left them, unaware that the safest building in London is a room full of reams. She thinks of the broad man in the brown coat, walking her to the door. Some debts are paid in coin. This one will be paid in silence.)*
> *(That evening she sits with the campaign book and practices not writing the name. It is a discipline, like scales: the omission rehearsed until it is natural. Creech. She writes "the warehouse" instead, and "the merchant," and once, without thinking, "the broad man in the brown coat." She smiles, and leaves it. The book will know who he is. No one else needs to. Some entries are safest in paraphrase.)*
> **Turns (8):**

### L3.S10 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the mirror — the reveal coda; substance, worn; the business dress's quiet power.*
> *(The mirror moment: morning light through the bedroom shutters, the severe gown — charcoal serge, plain as a column — and something lands in her chest like a small cold stone. Beauty has been her instrument for two Seasons, and it has served her well. But standing here, dressed for a warehouse, she understands it was never her* only *instrument. The maid was right to doubt the serge, and wrong: it is plain, and it is certain, and the two are not enemies. Augusta, seeing her on the stairs, does something unprecedented — she stops, actually stops, mid-step. "Creech will take you seriously," she says. It is the highest praise Augusta has for clothing, and they both know it.)*
> *("I would take you seriously," says Letitia faintly from the doorway, clutching the doorframe in her wrapper, "and I am* family *— imagine what a paper merchant will do." The gown does its work before Rose opens her mouth — which is the whole of fashion's power, and the reason Élise will one day rule London: to have spoken before speaking. The ballroom gown argued beauty. The invisible gown argued nothing. This one — dark as ink, plain as a ledger column — argues credibility. Three gowns, three arguments. She is collecting them the way other girls collect compliments.)*
> *(She practices the walk to the carriage — exact, brief, believed. Not the ballroom glide, not the invisible morning's hurry-past. A woman going to do business walks as if the pavement were a ledger and her feet were figures: placed, not posed. The footman holds the door. The October air is sharp. Somewhere across the square, a curtain twitches — the town, watching her go to a warehouse in serge, baffled and interested in equal measure. Let it watch. The gown has already said everything worth saying.)*
> *(The maid fastens the last pin and steps back, and there is a small silence. It is the particular silence of a servant who wants to say something and is deciding whether she may. "Miss," she says at last. "You look like someone it would be a mistake to underestimate." Rose meets her own eyes in the glass. "That," she says, "is the whole of the gown." The maid nods, satisfied, and goes down to tell the cook, because servants always tell the cook. By noon the whole house will know: Miss Hartwell looks like a mistake it would be unwise to make.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Take the realization — "never her only instrument." → Remembered:Her expanded arsenal; credibility joins beauty.
> 2. [examine]Read the gown — "dark as ink, plain as a ledger column." → Remembered:The credibility gown's thesis; she'll dress it again.
> 3. [social]Receive Augusta's nod — "single, sharp, definitive." → Remembered:Augusta's standard; it follows her down the stairs.
> 4. [social]Take Letitia's blessing — "do come back with all the reams." → Remembered:Letitia's wit; the family's wit steadies her.
> 5. [stance]Practice the opposite — "exact, brief, believed." → Remembered:Her anti-performance; the warehouse's requirement.
> 6. [examine]Keep "to have spoken before speaking." → Remembered:Her thesis of fashion; it governs every ritual after.
> 7. [micro]Become the third woman — complete. → Remembered:The third woman; the season's gowns are a trilogy.
> 8. [stance]Carry "arithmetic is the mother tongue." → Remembered:The mother tongue; she'll speak it to Creech.

### L3.S11 · [D] · Rose, Augusta
*Purpose: the figures, reported — the general sees the supply; the war's economics, updated.*
> *(The morning room, after breakfast. Rose lays it out the way Quill taught her to lay out a brief: in order, without emphasis, the figures doing the shouting so she doesn't have to. "Four times the paper a penny sheet should take. Good stock, not penny stock — the kind a quarterly review buys. Paid in gold, by a clerk's hand — a thin man, scarred knuckle. Tripled since the Hartwell numbers began." Augusta listens without moving, the way she listens to everything — as if stillness were note-taking.)*
> *("Gold." Augusta turns the word over. "Not Pyke's gold." A pause. "Gold is* someone else's*." She says it the way she says all the true things: simply, and at half volume, as if loudness would cheapen it.)*
> *("A thin man with a scarred knuckle, by Creech's description. The coin's weight, counted in a private book — every sovereign." Rose folds her hands. "And Creech's word at the door, Grandmama: 'do it in the open. It is the only thing the gutter cannot arrange.'")*
> *("Creech is a philosopher." Augusta rises, crosses to the campaign book, and closes it — gently, the way one closes a book one intends to reopen. "The purse has a* hand*, child. A scarred knuckle. Not a face — not yet. But no longer merely a blank. It is a* hand *that counts gold on a paper merchant's desk." She looks at Rose. "You have done in a morning what Lavinia has not done in years. Do you understand what you have? Not evidence —* economics*. A libel that costs this much is not a grudge. A grudge is cheap. This is an* investment*. And investments expect a* return*.")*
> *("The return being?" Rose asks, though she knows.)*
> *("Your ruin." Augusta says it simply. "Priced, to the ream. Counted, to the sovereign. Someone is* investing *in your destruction, child — and investors, unlike grudges, do not stop until they are paid.")*
> *(Rose sits very still. Priced to the ream. Somewhere in London a clerk with a scarred knuckle is counting out the next installment of her ruin, edges up, in tens, and the men behind him expect their money's worth. She thinks of the warehouse ticking in its shafts of light. She thinks: then we had better be worth more than we cost.)*

> 1. [examine]Brief the figures — "four times," "good stock," "gold," "tripled." → Remembered:Her report; the general briefs cleanly.
> 2. [examine]Hear "not Pyke's gold — someone else's"; the distinction. → Remembered:The distinction; it's the war's economics.
> 3. [social]Receive "done in a morning what Lavinia has not done in years." → Remembered:Augusta's praise; it's rare and therefore real.
> 4. [examine]Take "not evidence — economics"; the reframe. → Remembered:The reframe; economics is the higher evidence.
> 5. [examine]Weigh "a grudge is cheap; this is an investment." → Remembered:The sentence; it sets the war's scale.
> 6. [stance]Hear "your ruin, priced to the ream" — simply, the true thing. → Remembered:The true thing; it's the war's price tag.
> 7. [examine]Understand "investors do not stop until they are paid"; the warning. → Remembered:The warning; it defines the enemy's persistence.

### L3.S12 · [D] · Rose, Élise
*Purpose: the atelier hears everything — a Lantern man's coat, measured at Élise's; fashion as intelligence.*
> *(The atelier smells of pressed linen and hot irons, and the workroom hums the way workrooms do — pins, scissors, the low murmur of girls who talk while they sew and sew while they talk. Élise meets Rose at the door with the particular expression of a woman holding a secret too good to carry alone. "Miss Hartwell." She drops her voice, though the nearest girl is three yards off. "You will never* guess *who was measured here on Tuesday. For a coat. A* clerk's *coat.")*
> *("Tell me," Rose says, "and I shall not have to guess.")*
> *("A* clerk*, miss. From a* newspaper*." Élise holds up her own hand, indicating the knuckle. "Thin. Scarred knuckle — here. I notice hands; it is my trade. You cannot fit a sleeve without studying the hand, miss. The hand tells you where the coat will pull.")*
> *(Rose goes very still. The workroom hums on around them, oblivious.)*
> *("He ordered a coat, miss. Paid in* coin *— gold, miss, sovereigns, for a* clerk's *coat." Élise's eyes are bright. "Who pays gold for a clerk's coat? No clerk I ever measured. And while my girl measured him he talked — the way men talk when they think no one is listening, which in an atelier is* always*, because men think modistes are furniture." She leans closer. "He talked about* reams*, miss. About quantities. About a paper that buys like a quarterly and sells for a penny.")*
> *("Élise." Rose keeps her voice level with an effort that costs her. The workroom — the pins, the irons, the murmuring girls — is suddenly the most important room in London. "What did he say, exactly? Word for word, if you can.")*
> *("He said — " Élise's voice drops to a whisper. "— 'the purse is deep, but the purse is* impatient*.' Those were his words, miss." She fishes in her apron pocket and produces a scrap of paper, creased and precious. "And a modiste who does not write things down will be poor — so I wrote it, miss, on a scrap, the way I write a hem.")*
> *(Rose takes the scrap. The handwriting is careful and exact, the letters formed the way hems are sewn: small, even, meant to hold. She reads it twice. Then she looks at Élise — the bright eyes, the pins in her mouth, the fierce competence — and understands that the campaign has just been handed its second weapon, by a girl with a needle.)*

> 1. [examine]Hear "a clerk's coat" — the Tuesday measuring. → Remembered:The coincidence; the trail's gift.
> 2. [examine]Take "I notice hands; it is my trade." → Remembered:Élise's doctrine; it mirrors Creech's — hands are the text.
> 3. [micro]Go very still. → Remembered:The recognition made visible.
> 4. [examine]Ask "word for word, if you can." → Remembered:Her precision; the workroom becomes the most important room.

> 6. [social]Honor "a modiste who does not write things down will be poor." → Remembered:Élise's maxim; the intelligence doctrine.

### L3.S13 · [D] · Rose, Élise
*Purpose: "the purse is impatient" — what impatience means; the atelier as listening post, confirmed.*
> *("The purse is impatient." Rose turns the scrap over in her fingers. The paper is thin, the creases sharp. "Élise — this is* gold*. Do you understand what you have? Not the coat. The* sentence*.")*
> *("I understand I have a clerk with a scarred knuckle who pays in gold and talks too much, miss." Élise says it briskly, the way she says everything — but there is something underneath the briskness, a steadiness Rose has not seen in her before. "And I understand that my workroom hears everything. The* ton's *secrets come in with the hems. The* gutter's *secrets come in with the coats. Ladies talk while they're pinned. Clerks talk while they're measured." She shrugs, a small Gallic movement. "It is the whole of my fortune.")*
> *("It may be the whole of* mine*, Élise." Rose stops. Asking a modiste to spy is asking a great deal — it is asking a girl to risk the livelihood she has sewn together stitch by stitch. She chooses her words the way she chooses pins: carefully, and once. "Will you — will you tell me, when he comes back? Not to spy. I would not ask you to spy. Only to* notice*. You notice everything anyway. Only — tell* me *what you notice.")*
> *(Élise looks at her for a long moment, and there is something fierce and young in her face — the fierceness of the loyal, which is the fiercest thing there is. "Miss," she says, "the* Lantern *called you a fraud in print, for a penny. My atelier does not dress frauds." She picks up her shears. "It dresses* you*. I shall notice everything, miss — every clerk, every coat, every word about reams or purses. It will be my* pleasure*." A pause. "And the other modistes hear things too. The whole of London is measured* somewhere* — Bond Street, the City, the lanes. We talk, miss. Girls with needles always talk.")*
> *(Rose walks home through the October afternoon with the scrap in her reticule and Élise's fierce face in her mind. A listening post, confirmed — not built, but* found*, the way the best things in this war have been found: by accident, by a girl with pins in her mouth. She thinks of Creech's warehouse, ticking. She thinks of the atelier, humming. Two rooms in London now, both listening. The purse is deep, and the purse is impatient — and impatience, she is learning, talks.)*

> 1. [examine]Name it "gold" — "not the coat." → Remembered:Her valuation; intelligence is the gold.

### L3.S14 · [D] · Rose, Lavinia
*Purpose: the clerk, reported — Lavinia hears about the scarred knuckle; the guide's excitement.*
> *(Lavinia's drawing room is all amber and mirrors at four o'clock, and Lavinia herself is at her glittering best — which is to say, at her most dangerous. Rose tells it straight: the warehouse, the private book, the atelier, the coat, the scrap. She watches Lavinia's face as the pieces land. "A scarred knuckle!" Lavinia cries, coming half out of her chair. "Oh,* wonderful*. Do you know what that means, darling? Do you* understand *what you have?")*
> *("That Creech and Élise described the same man," Rose says. "The clerk who counts gold on the warehouse desk is the clerk who orders coats at the atelier. Thin. Scarred knuckle. Talks too much when he thinks no one is listening.")*
> *(Lavinia goes still — the glitter pausing mid-air, which Rose has learned means the guide is actually thinking. "Larkin said he knew the* hand *that writes the notes with the extra pay." She looks at Rose. "I* wonder*, darling — I* wonder *if it is the* same *hand. The clerk's hand. The scarred knuckle.")*
> *("One hand," Rose says slowly, working it out aloud. "Carrying gold to Creech. Carrying notes to Larkin — notes with the extra pay. Gold for the reams." She looks up. "The purse's* errand boy*.")*
> *("The purse's* clerk*, darling." Lavinia corrects her gently, the way one corrects a promising pupil. "There is a difference, and it is the whole of the trail. Errand boys are* spent *— used up and thrown away. Clerks* talk*. Élise proved it." She rises, crosses to the window, all business now under the glitter. "Find the clerk. Follow the clerk. And the clerk will lead us — " A wave of the hand. "— well.* Somewhere*. Clerks always lead* somewhere*. That is the nature of clerks.")*
> *(Walking home, Rose thinks of the scarred knuckle — counting gold, writing notes, ordering coats — and understands that the blank center of her map now has a pulse. One hand. Gold to Creech, notes to Larkin. The same thin man. Hands belong to people, Augusta said once, years ago, about a groom with a thieving habit. People can be found. People can be followed. People — unlike purses — have addresses.)*

> 1. [examine]Hear "a scarred knuckle! Oh, wonderful." → Remembered:Lavinia's excitement; the guide loves the trail.

### L3.S15 · [F] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the scrap of paper — Élise's note, kept; a small diegetic choice, remembered.*
> *(Élise's scrap sits on her desk in the lamplight — the handwriting careful and exact: "the purse is deep, but the purse is* impatient*." Rose holds it to the lamp and studies it the way she studied the Bath maps: the slope of the letters, the pressure of the pen, the small blot where the girl hurried. The young modiste, pins in her mouth, writing down what a clerk said during an inseam. The campaign's most human intelligence — not a ledger column, not a map line, but a girl's courage, creased and precious.)*
> *(The drawer is becoming an archive —* Lanterns*,* Gazette *cuttings, maps, and now this. The scrap belongs with the archive: filed, preserved, shown to Quill when the heavy weapon is readied. Or with her — carried close in the reticule, the words working on her like a motto. Impatient, impatient. The enemy's temperament, her ally; a reminder that haste makes mistakes and she must only wait to collect them. Or nowhere at all — burned, the ash in the grate, because Élise's handwriting, found among the papers of a woman at war with a penny press, would end the girl's livelihood. Possibly the girl. Small choices, Augusta says, make the general — the big ones only reveal her.)*
> *(She holds the scrap over the lamp for a moment — close enough to feel the heat on her fingers — and chooses.)*
> *(The lamp gutters. Outside, the square is quiet with the particular quiet of a London street after midnight, when even the watchmen have found doorways. She thinks of Élise's face as she handed over the scrap: the bright eyes, the fierce pride, the girl standing a little straighter because a lady had asked for her noticing and meant it. "A modiste who does not write things down will be poor." Poor in coin, the girl meant. But there are other kinds of poor, and Élise, pins in her mouth and ink on the scrap, is rich in the kind that matters.)*
> *(She thinks of what the scrap cost to obtain: nothing but attention, paid gladly. And what it might cost if found: everything the girl has sewn together, stitch by stitch. The atelier, the girls, the future. The arithmetic is not difficult. Small choices make the general, Augusta says. The big ones only reveal her. She holds the scrap over the lamp, close enough to feel the heat, far enough to choose, and chooses.)*
*Choices (no coin cost; the game remembers):*
- **Pin it to the campaign book** — evidence, filed with the maps. *(Information+; Quill will want to see it.)*
- **Carry it with her** — the words, kept close; a reminder of what impatience costs. *(Resolve+.)*
- **Burn it** — Élise's handwriting must never be found among her papers. *(Honor+; Élise, protected.)*

> 1. [examine]Hold the scrap to the lamp — the handwriting "careful and exact." → Remembered:The scrap's physicality; it's the campaign's most human intelligence.

> 3. [examine]Weigh carrying — "the words working on her like a motto." → Remembered:The motto's work; impatience as her ally, kept close.

### L3.S16 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the decision, made — ride the courier line herself; the night's journey, planned.*
> *(She stands at the window as dusk comes down over the square, and makes the decision the chapter has been building toward — not with drama, but with the quiet click of a well-made lock. Not Lavinia's version: Lavinia would make it a lark, a midnight adventure with a flask. Not an agent's report, read second-hand at breakfast. Hers — on horseback, in the dark, the way she learned how money travels back in the spring: by* moving *with it. She learned the Park's rides by riding them. She learned the money's roads by walking them. The warehouse showed her the supply. The coffee-houses showed her the distribution. Now she will* ride *the line between them — from Creech's reams to Pyke's press to Larkin's corner — and learn it the way she learns everything: by doing, by being there, by* moving*.)*
> *(She sends a note to Lavinia by the evening post: "Tonight. The courier line. Alone." Lavinia's reply comes within the hour — four words, in the precise hand, on Crane stationery: "Wear boots, darling. And* tell no one*." Rose smiles — the smile of a general whose orders have been understood before they were fully given. She was not going to tell anyone. Not Augusta — who would forbid it, or worse, approve it and insist on coming. Not Letitia — who would insist on coming and bring sandwiches. Some rides are for the rider alone.)*
> *(She lays out the habit herself — the dark riding dress, the boots, the plain hat — and tells the maid she is dining early and retiring with a headache, which is a lie, and the first lie she has told in this war, and she finds it sits surprisingly lightly. Midnight. The stable yard. The courier herself. She stands at the window a moment longer, watching the lamps come on in the square, and feels the particular calm that comes before a thing she has decided to do: the quiet after the lock clicks.)*
> *(The house settles around her with the particular settling of a London house at night: timbers cooling, the servants' stair gone quiet. She stands at the window and watches the square's lamps, and thinks of the line she will ride. The warehouse dark. The press lit yellow. The depot behind the chandler's. She rode the Park's rides blindfolded, for a wager, at fifteen. She walked the money's roads in the spring. Tonight she rides the libel's road.)*

> 1. [stance]Make the decision — "the quiet click of a well-made lock." → Remembered:Her resolve; the chapter's hinge clicks shut.
> 2. [examine]Choose hers — "not Lavinia's version, not an agent's report." → Remembered:Her ownership; the ride is hers alone.
> 3. [examine]Take the method — "by moving with it"; knowledge as kinetic. → Remembered:Her method; knowledge is kinetic.

### L3.S17 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: midnight — the stables; the ride begins; the courier herself.*
> *(Midnight. The stable yard is silver and silent — the moon high, the cobbles shining, the horses shifting in their boxes with soft blowing sounds. Her mare is saddled and waiting, stamping softly, her breath coming in pale clouds. Rose mounts from the block the way she has mounted ten thousand times, settles into the familiar creak of leather, and rides out through the sleeping streets with the groom's lantern bobbing behind her until the mews gate, where she waves him back. Alone from here. The arrangement, the lie, the headache — all behind her now.)*
> *(London at midnight is a different city. The bells are silent. The hawkers are gone, their barrows wheeled away. The coffee-houses are dark, the clubs shuttered, the great houses blind. Only the* press *is awake — she can hear it three streets off, the thump and rattle of the night shift, the gutter's heartbeat. Paper to press to hawker. She is going to ride the whole of it — the libel's journey, in reverse and then forward — and learn it the way she learned how money travels: not by reading about it, but by* moving *with it, the mare's hoofbeats soft on the sleeping streets.)*
> *(She reins in at the corner opposite the printing house and watches from the shadows. The windows are lit yellow. Figures move behind them — the night shift, shirtsleeved, ink to the elbow. A boy carries stacks to the door; the paper goes in white and comes out printed, the libel being born in lamplight while the city sleeps. She sits very still, the mare quiet beneath her, and watches the whole damp machinery of her ruin at work. It is only machinery: rooms, machines, tired men, ink. Machinery can be learned. What can be learned can be ridden. What can be ridden can be beaten. She turns the mare's head toward the depot, and follows the cart at a distance, silent as smoke.)*
> *(The groom holds the mare at the mounting block, half asleep on his feet, and asks no questions. It is the particular discretion of servants who have seen stranger things than midnight rides, though he cannot imagine what. "Back by dawn, miss," he murmurs. "The mare knows the way." She does. They all do, the good ones. The mare's ears prick at the empty street, and they move off together, horse and rider, silver and silence, into the sleeping city.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [micro]Mount at midnight — the silver yard, the stamping mare. → Remembered:The departure; the ride begins in silver and silence.
> 2. [examine]See the different city — bells silent, hawkers gone. → Remembered:The midnight city; it's the gutter's other face.
> 3. [examine]Hear the press — "the thump, the rattle"; the heartbeat. → Remembered:The press's sound; the gutter's heartbeat at night.
> 4. [stance]Watch "the libel, being born" — stacks in white, out printed. → Remembered:The witnessing; she saw her ruin manufactured.
> 5. [examine]Pair the sounds — the day's bell and the night's thump; "two sounds, one war." → Remembered:The pairing; day and night are one war.
> 6. [examine]Learn "it is only machinery" — rooms, machines, tired men, ink. → Remembered:The demystification; the great weapon is a room.
> 7. [stance]Take the chain — "learned, ridden, beaten." → Remembered:Her logic; it's the campaign's syllogism.

### L3.S18 · [T] · Rose (observing)
*Purpose: the press at night — the chapel; compositors, ink, the stone; the libel, being made.*
> *(The printing house at close range is smaller than she expected, and meaner. A low room, yellow-lit, the air thick with the smell of hot metal and lamp oil and damp paper. The press itself — the great iron beast — thumps and clanks in the center like a heart, and around it move the chapel: compositors with ink to the elbow, setting type by feel in the bad light; a boy feeding paper, white sheets going in; another boy taking them away, printed, the black accusation fresh and shining. Ink on the stone. Ink on the fingers. Ink in the air, in the lungs, under the nails for a week.)*
> *(She has seen the distribution — the coffee-houses, the clubs, the corners. She has seen the supply — the warehouse, the ledgers, Creech's scarred desk. Now she is seeing the* middle *— the press, the chapel, where paper becomes libel, where white reams become black accusations. The gutter's great weapon — the thing that has cost her sleep, that has named her friends dupes, that has set the ton mentioning over breakfast — and it is* this*: a room, a machine, some tired men, and ink. She watches until the cart is loaded in the court below — the stacks tied with twine, the driver flicking the reins, the horse blowing steam in the cold.)*
> *(Then she follows — at a distance, on horseback, silent, the mare's hoofbeats soft on the sleeping streets. The depot: a yard behind a chandler's, sleepy men, the division of the stacks, the boys gathering with their hands out, the extra-pay notes slipped into pockets along with the papers. Larkin among them — she thinks it is Larkin; the voice, the stance, the professional eagerness even at this hour. Paper to press to hawker. She rides the line — the libel's journey, in reverse and then forward, the whole of it, in one night — and the city arranges itself around her like a map being drawn.)*
> *(A compositor looks up as she passes the window's edge. He is a boy, really, ink to the elbow, his face grey with lamplight, and for a moment their eyes meet across the yard. He looks away first, back to his type. The moment is too brief to matter. But it stays with her: the grey face, the tired eyes, the hands moving by feel. These are the hands that set the type that named her a fraud. They are only hands, young and tired and ink-stained. She files the face with the others. The clerk. The warehousemen. The boys. The war's cast, assembling.)*

> 1. [examine]See the middle — "where paper becomes libel." → Remembered:The transmutation; it's the war's alchemy.
> 2. [examine]Take "smaller than she expected; meaner" — the room. → Remembered:The reality; great weapons are small rooms.
> 3. [micro]Watch till the cart is loaded — the stacks tied; the patience. → Remembered:Her patience; she watches till the work is done.
> 4. [examine]Note the depot — sleepy men, division, notes in pockets. → Remembered:The routine; the gutter's work is ordinary.
> 5. [stance]Follow — at a distance, silent; the pursuit. → Remembered:Her pursuit; the line is ridden, not just mapped.
> 6. [examine]Ride "the libel's journey, in reverse, then forward" — the whole. → Remembered:The whole journey; it's known by heart now.
> 7. [social]Note Larkin among them — the voice, the stance; the recognition. → Remembered:Larkin's presence; the night confirms the geography.

### L3.S19 · [D] · Rose (reflecting)
*Purpose: the ride — the circuit, ridden; how libels travel, learned the way she learned how money travels.*
> *(She follows the cart through the dark streets — Larkin among the boys, she is nearly certain now; the voice, the stance, the professional eagerness even at this hour. And as she rides, the night teaches its lesson, the way nights do: plainly, without rhetoric. It is* work *— done by hands, for coin, in the dark. The libel is not a thunderbolt. It is a job of work, performed by tired men for extra pay, the way docks are loaded and streets are swept. And work can be interrupted. Hands can be bought. Coin can be traced — she traced it to Creech's desk, edges up, in tens.)*
> *(She thinks of the scarred knuckle, counting gold on the scarred desk. She thinks of the clerk's coat, measured at Élise's — the inseam, the talk, the purse that is deep and impatient. She thinks of the purse behind it all, paying for the sky over this gutter, paying for the ink and the boys and the extra-pay notes. The cart turns a corner. The boys scatter to their corners, the notes in their pockets, the papers under their arms. Dawn is coming — the sky going grey over the rooftops, the lamps going out one by one like candles being pinched.)*
> *(She turns her mare for home. She has ridden the courier line — paper to press to hawker, one night — and she knows it now the way she knows the Park's rides: by* heart*. Not mapped. Not reported.* Known*. The mare's pace quickens on the familiar streets; the city is waking up around her, shutters going up, the first carts rattling. The whole sleeping city seems, for a moment, like a map she has finally learned to read — every corner annotated, every depot marked, every hand accounted for. She rides in through the mews gate as the sky goes pink, and the groom takes the mare with a look that asks nothing. Some lies, she reflects, unsaddling in her mind if not in fact, are worth the telling.)*
> *(At the mews gate the sky is the color of wet slate, and the first sparrows are arguing in the eaves. She dismounts, her legs stiff, and leads the mare in herself. The groom is still half asleep. The yard is silver-grey. In the stable the mare blows softly and noses at her pocket for the apple that is always there. Some conspiracies, Rose reflects, handing it over, are conducted entirely in apples.)*
> **Turns (8):**
> 1. [examine]Learn "it is work — done by hands, for coin, in the dark." → Remembered:The night's lesson; the libel is labor.
> 2. [examine]Take the vulnerabilities — "interrupted, bought, traced." → Remembered:The vulnerabilities; work has weak points.
> 3. [micro]Turn the mare for home at dawn — the sky grey. → Remembered:The dawn return; the ride completes.
> 4. [stance]Know it "by heart — not mapped, not reported, known." → Remembered:Her knowing; it's deeper than maps.
> 5. [examine]Feel the intimacy — "not information, but the feel." → Remembered:The feel; the machinery has a texture.
> 6. [examine]Keep "the libel is not a paragraph; it is work." → Remembered:The sentence; it's the night's thesis.
> 7. [social]Carry Larkin's presence — the confirmation. → Remembered:The confirmation; the night's cast is known.
> 8. [examine]Read the city — "a map she has finally learned to read." → Remembered:Her literacy; London is legible now.

### L3.S20 · plot beat · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — THE COURIER RIDE, AND WHAT COMES NEXT.*
> *(The ride is ridden. Dawn has come and gone, and Rose has slept three hours and woken with the map still behind her eyes. The question now is what the riding* buys *— a general does not ride for the pleasure of riding, though there was pleasure in it, the honest pleasure of a dawn canter through empty streets. She rides to* learn*, and having learned, she must decide what the learning is* for*. Three roads lie open. Ride it again, deeper — the press itself, inside, among the compositors, where the type is set; Lavinia will say "deeper, darling," and Augusta will say "deeper is where the danger lives," and both will be right, which is the trouble. Hand it to agents — hire riders, build the network, return to the unimpeachable lady at headquarters; the information second-hand, the danger delegated. Or ride the Park's own lines — turn the lesson homeward, rebuild Hartwell's own courier lines, let the Park's news travel its own roads; the stables, the tenants, the estate's own intelligence, the B-thread seeded and beginning.)*
> *(She sits at the desk with the cold breakfast she has not touched, and weighs them the way Augusta weighs dispatches: by what each costs and what each buys. The choice sits on the desk beside the untouched eggs like a drawn blade — the war's next phase, chosen in daylight, after breakfast, the way generals choose things.)*
> *(She thinks of the press at midnight: the yellow windows, the ink to the elbow, the boy with the grey face. She thinks of Lavinia's four words: "Wear boots, darling. And tell no one." She thinks of Augusta's stillness in the morning room: "You will not ride it alone again." Deeper would take her inside, among the compositors, where the type is set and the danger lives. Agents would build her a network: riders in the dark, reports at breakfast, the information second-hand but the danger delegated. The Park's own lines would turn the lesson homeward: the stables, the tenants, Hartwell's own couriers, the B-thread seeded. Three roads. One night's learning. The eggs, she reflects, are the only thing in the room not waiting for her decision.)*
*★ KEY DECISION 2/3 — The courier ride:*
- **Ride it again, deeper** — *Information+; risk deepens.* Next time: the press itself — inside, among the compositors, where the type is set. *(Lavinia: "Deeper, darling!"; Augusta: "Deeper is where the danger lives.")*
- **Hand it to agents** — *Safety+; the network, built.* Hire riders to watch the line; Rose returns to the unimpeachable lady, commanding from headquarters. *(Augusta approves; the information will be second-hand.)*
- **Ride the Park's own lines** — *Estate+; the stables, claimed.* Turn the lesson homeward — rebuild Hartwell's own courier lines; the Park's news will travel its own roads. *(The B-thread, seeded: the stables season begins.)*
> *(The game remembers the choice — and the means: the deeper ride, the agents' network, the Park's own lines. The destination was never in doubt. The signature is hers.)*

> 1. [examine]Ask what the riding buys — "a general does not ride for pleasure." → Remembered:Her audit; the ride must pay.
> 2. [stance]Hear Lavinia — "deeper, darling!"; the temptation. → Remembered:Lavinia's appetite; it's the guide's nature.
> 3. [stance]Hear Augusta — "deeper is where the danger lives." → Remembered:Augusta's warning; both are right.
> 4. [examine]Price the agents' road — "second-hand" information. → Remembered:The cost of distance; second-hand is the price.
> 5. [examine]Consider the Park's lines — "the B-thread, seeded." → Remembered:The homeward option; the stables season begins.

### L3.S21 · [D] · Rose, Letitia
*Purpose: the aunt discovers the ride — comedy; the worry, real; being known by one's people.*
> *("Where were you last night?" Letitia asks, over the breakfast things — and her voice has the particular pitch it gets when she has rehearsed a question all morning and is very proud of the rehearsal. "Between midnight and dawn, darling? Because the house was quiet, and your bed was — " She stops. "Your bed was* empty*, darling. I looked. I could not sleep, and I looked.")*
> *("Aunt Letitia." Rose considers lying, and discards it — Letitia's face, when lied to, does something complicated and heartbreaking that is not worth the price of any secret. "I rode the courier line. From Creech's warehouse to Pyke's press to Larkin's corner. On horseback. At midnight. In the dark.")*
> *(Letitia stares. Her hand goes to her throat. "Midnight," she says faintly. "On horseback. In the* dark*." A pause. "Were you — were you* seen*?")*
> *("No one saw," Rose says. "No one follows well at midnight. That is rather the point of midnight." She takes Letitia's hand. "Aunt — the libel is* work*. Done by hands, for coin, in the dark. I rode it to learn it. And I learned it — by heart, the way I know the Park's rides.")*
> *("Work," Letitia repeats, weakly. "For* coin*." She sits down rather suddenly. "Darling — the* danger*. The* dark*. You might have been — " She does not finish. She does not need to. The unfinished sentence sits between them like a third person at the table.)*
> *("I know," Rose says quietly. "And I am not sorry. But I tell you because you looked, and because you could not sleep, and because I would rather you knew and worried than not knew and — " She stops too. The unfinished sentences are piling up. "Knowledge is the only excuse for midnight, Aunt. It had better be worth it. I intend to make it worth it.")*
> *(Letitia looks at her for a long moment — and then, to Rose's astonishment, laughs: a small, helpless, genuine laugh. "My niece," she says. "Riding courier lines at midnight. Your mother would have — " She stops, shakes her head. "Take someone next time, darling. That is all I ask. Take someone, and come back, and tell me* everything *over breakfast, because I shall not sleep until you do.")*

> 1. [social]Face the question — "where were you last night?"; the confrontation. → Remembered:The confrontation; honesty is the only defense.
> 2. [examine]Consider lying — and discard it; Letitia's face. → Remembered:Her honesty; the aunt's face is not worth a lie.
> 3. [examine]Take "your bed was empty, darling" — the looking. → Remembered:The looking; Letitia's worry is surveillance of the loving kind.
> 4. [social]Tell it straight — "I rode the courier line"; the confession. → Remembered:Her confession; the aunt is told everything.
> 5. [examine]Hear "midnight, on horseback, in the dark" — the faintness. → Remembered:Letitia's faintness; the comedy is real worry's costume.
> 6. [social]Take the hand — "I tell you because you looked"; the tenderness. → Remembered:Her tenderness; being known is the price of being loved.
> 7. [examine]Hear the laugh — "small, helpless, genuine"; the surprise. → Remembered:The laugh; worry and pride can coexist.
> 8. [social]Receive "take someone next time" — the condition. → Remembered:The condition; the aunt's terms are accepted.

### L3.S22 · [D] · Rose, Augusta
*Purpose: the courier's report — the general hears the night; the campaign's geography, complete.*
> *(Augusta hears the report in the morning room, and her face does what it does when a dispatch contains both good news and bad: nothing at all. Rose tells it straight — the stables at midnight, the sleeping streets, the press lit yellow, the chapel with ink to the elbow, the cart, the depot, Larkin among the boys, the whole line ridden in one night. "The depot," Augusta says, when Rose finishes. "A yard behind a chandler's. Sleepy men." A pause. "You* rode *it. In the* dark*.")*
> *("I rode it," Rose says. "To learn it. And I learned it — by heart.")*
> *(Augusta is silent for a long moment. Then: "The gutter's roads run both ways, child. You have now* walked *them both ways." She rises, crosses to the campaign book, opens it to the maps. "Paper to press to hawker. You mapped the supply in Bath. You have now mapped the crime in London." She looks up. "Do you understand what you have done? Not the information — the* demonstration*. You have shown that the libel is* work*. Done by hands, for coin. And work can be interrupted. Hands can be bought. Coin can be traced.")*
> *("I know," Rose says. "The clerk's hand. The scarred knuckle. One hand, two errands — gold to Creech, notes to Larkin.")*
> *("One hand." Augusta closes the book. "And behind the hand — " She stops. The sentence hangs unfinished, the way the important ones do. "— the* purse*. The purse is deep, and the purse is impatient. Élise's sentence." A pause. "We follow the hand. We do not* break *it — not yet. Hands that count gold can be made to count other things. Hands belong to people, child. People can be turned.")*
> *(Rose sits very still. The morning room is quiet. Outside, London goes about its business, unaware that its nights have been mapped. "Not the roads," she says slowly, testing it. "The hands.")*
> *("Not the roads," Augusta agrees. "The hands.")*
> *(Augusta pours the chocolate herself, the general's hands steady, and for a moment the morning room is only a morning room: the silver pot, the steam, the October light. Then she sets down the pot, and the general returns. "You rode it alone," she says. It is not a question. "You will not ride it alone again." It is not a request. Rose opens her mouth, and closes it. Some orders are gifts in disguise. "Lavinia," Augusta says, "rides with you next time. The guide knows the town's nights. That is why she is the guide.")*

### L3.S23 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the hand, considered — the clerk's humanity; the campaign's conscience, awake.*
> *(She thinks of the clerk on the walk home — the thin man with the scarred knuckle, counting gold on Creech's desk, ordering coats at Élise's, talking too much during an inseam. A man with a scarred knuckle and a clerk's coat and a tongue that runs away from him. A man who pays in gold he does not own, for a paper he does not read, on behalf of a purse he does not see. She has spent the chapter learning his hands. She has not spent a moment learning* him *— and the omission sits in her like a stone in a shoe.)*
> *(Augusta said: we do not break him. Not yet. Hands that count gold can be made to count other things. But the sentence has a shadow, and the shadow is this: the clerk is a* person*. He has a coat that needed measuring. He has talk in him — the talkative kind, the lonely kind, the kind that fills an atelier's silence because his own rooms are silent. Cornering him would be easy now — the warehouse knows his schedule, the atelier knows his measurements, the night has shown his roads. Easy, and* wrong *— or not wrong, but* costly*: the cost measured not in coin but in the thing she is trying to keep clean. Her name. Her hands. The difference between her and the purse is not the money. It is* this *— the pause before the cornering, the stone in the shoe, the refusal to treat a person as a means.)*
> *(She writes in the campaign book, in the small tight hand: "THE HAND — one hand, two errands. Follow, do not break. Hands belong to people." She underlines "people" twice. Then she sits back, and looks at the word, and understands that the campaign has just acquired a conscience with a name attached to it — not an abstraction, but a thin man with a scarred knuckle who talks too much. Some enemies are ideas. This one is a person. Persons must be* handled* — Augusta's word, and the right one. Handled, not broken. Followed, not cornered. The distinction is the whole of the difference between her and them, and she intends to keep it.)*

> 1. [examine]Consider the clerk — the coat, the talk, the loneliness; the person. → Remembered:Her humanization; the hand acquires a person.
> 2. [stance]Feel the omission — "a stone in a shoe"; the conscience. → Remembered:The stone; conscience has a texture.

*Purpose: white roses — the floriography; the clean name, spoken in flowers.*
> *(Letitia comes into the morning room with a posy in her hands and the particular expression of a woman who has been to the flower market and is very pleased with herself. "Darling," she says. "White roses. In* bud*." She holds them out. "For the house. For — " She stops, suddenly shy. "For* you*." )*
> *("White roses," Rose says. She takes the posy. The buds are tight and pale, the thorns sharp under her thumb. "In* bud*." She looks at her aunt. "The clean name, Aunt? In the flower language?")*
> *("The clean name," Letitia confirms — and her voice has the particular firmness it gets when she is sure of a thing, which is rare enough to be precious. "Not yet opened, darling. In* bud*. The name is not clean* yet *— the* Lantern *still prints, the ton still mentions. But it is* coming *clean. Buds open, darling. That is what buds* do*.")*
> *(Rose holds the posy to her face. The buds have no scent yet — scent comes with the opening, the way everything good comes with the opening. She thinks of the chapter: the warehouse, the ledgers, the night ride, the scarred knuckle, the clerk's humanity, the firewall, the scrap. The name is not clean yet. But the buds are here, in her hands, pale and tight and certain. "In bud," she says. "Soon.")*
> *("Soon," Letitia agrees — and her voice has the particular brightness it gets when she has said exactly the right thing, which is rarer still. "I shall put them in water, darling. In the* hall*. Where everyone can see them." A pause. "The town will ask what they mean. Let them ask. Flowers are the one language the* Lantern *cannot print.")*
> *(The white roses go into water in the hall, in the tall vase, where the light falls on them. All day the callers come and go, and all day the buds sit there — pale, tight, unopened, certain. The ton asks. The ton is told. The ton repeats it, the way the ton repeats everything: white roses, in bud. The clean name. Coming.)*

### L3.S25 · [D] · Rose, Henry
*Purpose: Henry asks — Horse Guards has inquired about him; what loyalty requires.*
> *(Henry calls in the afternoon — not as a suitor, though the flowers in his hand suggest the habit persists, but as something else: an officer with a question he does not want to ask. He is very correct. He is very pale. He puts the flowers down without the usual ceremony, and Rose understands, before he speaks, that this is not a call. It is a* council*.)*
> *("Rose," he says. "Horse Guards has — " He stops. Starts again. "There has been an* inquiry*. About me. About my — " He gestures, vaguely, at the whole of the situation: the campaign, the* Lantern*, the midnight rides he does not know about and would not approve of if he did. "About my judgment. My* associations*." A pause. "My* loyalty*." )*
> *("Your loyalty," Rose repeats. The words sit in the room like a third person.)*
> *("To the service." Henry is very still. "There is a whisper — an old one, from the spring. That I have allowed a — a* private *attachment to compromise my — my* public *duty*." He looks at her directly, and the directness costs him; she can see it costing him. "They ask what my loyalty requires. Whether I will — " He stops. The sentence will not come. "Whether standing* beside *you is compatible with standing* in *the service.")*
> *(Rose looks at him — the correct officer, the pale face, the unfinished sentence — and understands that this is the question the Season has been circling: not what* she *requires of* him*, but what* loyalty *requires of* him*, the thing itself, the old military virtue, weighed against a woman with a campaign. She does not answer at once. The silence stretches. Somewhere in the house a clock ticks. The flowers sit on the table, suddenly absurd — the suitor's offering, in a council of war.)*
> *("Henry," she says at last. "What do* you *think it requires?")*
> *(He has come in uniform. Not full dress, but the undress uniform, the working clothes of an officer, and Rose understands the choice: he is here as Major Lord Henry Beaumont, not as a suitor. The distinction matters to him. It costs him something to maintain it; she can see the cost in the set of his shoulders, in the way his hand tightens once on his hat brim before he sets it down. The flowers, white roses from a good florist, sit between them like an apology for the uniform. Or the uniform like an apology for the flowers.)*

> 5. [stance]Refuse to answer at once — the silence; the respect. → Remembered:Her silence; the question deserves weight.

### L3.S26 · [D] · Rose, Henry
*Purpose: loyalty's shape — the old whisper; the cost of standing beside her; the question, clarified.*
> *("What do I think it requires?" Henry repeats. He stands — the officer's habit, thinking on his feet — and crosses to the window. "I think — " He stops. "There is a whisper, Rose. An old one. That my judgment is — *compromised*. That a private attachment has made me — " He turns. "—* unsafe*." A pause. "Do you understand what that word means, in the service?* Unsafe *is not* disloyal*. It is worse. Disloyal can be court-martialed. Unsafe is — " He gestures. "— quietly shelved. Promotions that do not come. Commands that go to other men.")*
> *("The cost," Rose says quietly. "Of standing beside me.")*
> *("The* price*." Henry corrects her gently — the officer's precision, even now. "Loyalty has a price, Rose. Mine is being* named*, in whispers, at Horse Guards." He crosses back, sits — the suitor's habit reasserting itself, the man returning inside the officer. "And yet — " He stops. "And yet I am* here*. Calling on you. In the afternoon. With flowers." A small, painful smile. "The inquiry has not* stopped *me, Rose. It has only — " He gestures again, vaguely. "— made me* ask*. What does loyalty require? Of* me*? Not of you — of* me*?")*
> *(Rose looks at him — the correct officer, the painful smile, the question asked twice — and understands the shape of it now: not a choice between her and the service, but a question about what loyalty* is*. Whether it is obedience — to the whisper, to the inquiry, to the safe path. Or whether it is something else: the thing itself, the old virtue, standing beside what is right even when the whisper says* unsafe*. She does not answer. She will not answer* for *him — that is the one thing she knows: the answer must be his, or it is not an answer.)*
> *("Henry," she says. "I will not decide this for you. I* cannot*. But I will tell you what I think loyalty* is *— if you want to hear it.")*
> *("I want to hear it," Henry says.)*
> *(A coal shifts in the grate. Somewhere below, a door closes. The afternoon waits, the way afternoons do when something real is being decided in them.)*

### L3.S27 · [D] · Rose, Henry
*Purpose: ★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — HENRY'S QUESTION.*
> *(The afternoon has gone quiet — the particular quiet of a London drawing room when something real is being decided. Henry sits very still, the flowers forgotten on the table, and waits. Rose looks at him — the correct officer, the pale face, the question asked twice — and understands that what she says now will be remembered: by him, by the inquiry, by the Season. Three roads lie open. Answer — tell him what she thinks loyalty is, plainly, the way Augusta tells true things: simply, and at half volume. Deflect — refuse the question's terms, send him back to Horse Guards with his own counsel; the honorable distance, the suitor kept at arm's length for his own good. Or ask — ask exactly what Horse Guards wants of him, what the inquiry's terms are, what "unsafe" would cost in practice; the lawyer's road, the facts before the philosophy.)*
> *(She thinks of Augusta: loyalty is not obedience. She thinks of the clerk: hands belong to people. She thinks of the campaign book, with "THE HAND" underlined twice. The choice sits on the table beside the forgotten flowers like a drawn blade — the Season's question, chosen in daylight, in a quiet room, the way the important things are chosen.)*
> *(She thinks of the flowers on the table, white roses from a good florist, suddenly absurd in a council of war. She thinks of Horse Guards: the inquiry, the whisper, the word "unsafe" hanging in the air like smoke. Answering would give him the plain truth, loyalty as she understands it, simply, at half volume. Deflecting would keep him at arm's length, the honorable distance, the suitor protected from the war for his own good. Asking would turn lawyer: the inquiry's terms, the cost of "unsafe" in practice, facts before philosophy. Three roads. One question, asked twice. The afternoon waits.)*
> *(She thinks of Augusta's verdict, still to come: whispers are the cheapest thing in London. She thinks of the campaign book, with "THE FOUR" waiting to be written tonight. She thinks of Henry's hand, which she has not yet taken, and the genuine smile she has not yet seen, and the officer standing at her window with his back to the room. Whatever she says now, he will carry it back to Horse Guards like a dispatch. The words must be chosen the way Augusta chooses hers: simply, at half volume, and once.)*
*★ KEY DECISION 3/3 — Henry's question:*
- **Answer** — *Loyalty+; the truth, plainly.* Tell him what she thinks loyalty is — simply, at half volume, the way true things are told. *(The honorable answer; it will be remembered.)*
- **Deflect** — *Distance+; the honorable arm's length.* Refuse the question's terms; send him back with his own counsel, for his own good. *(The safe road; the suitor kept at distance.)*
- **Ask** — *Clarity+; the lawyer's road.* Ask exactly what Horse Guards wants — the inquiry's terms, what "unsafe" costs in practice. *(The facts before the philosophy.)*
> *(The game remembers the choice — and the means: the plain answer, the honorable distance, the lawyer's questions. The Season's question was never going to be answered once. The signature is hers.)*

### L3.S28 · [D] · Rose, Henry
*Purpose: the answer, given — whatever was chosen, the honorable uncertainty; loyalty, defined by refraining.*
> *(She gives him the answer — the one she chose, in the quiet room, in daylight — and Henry hears it the way officers hear orders: completely, and without moving. When she finishes, he is silent for a long moment. Then he rises — the officer's habit — and crosses to the window, and stands there with his back to her, looking out at the square. "The courtesy," he says at last, "of not deciding for me." He turns. "Do you know what that* is*, Rose? In the service, everyone decides for you — Horse Guards, the whisper, the inquiry. You are the only person in this — " He gestures, vaguely, at the whole of it. "— who has* refrained*.")*
> *("Refrained," Rose repeats.)*
> *("Refrained." Henry crosses back, sits — the man returning inside the officer. "I came here asking what loyalty requires. You would not tell me." A small, genuine smile — the first real one of the afternoon. "And in the not-telling, you told me the only thing worth knowing: that loyalty is not obedience. Not to the whisper. Not to the inquiry. Not even to* you*." He takes her hand — the suitor's gesture, at last, in the council's aftermath. "I am* deciding*, Rose. Not decided —* deciding*. The inquiry will wait. Horse Guards will wait. The* deciding *is the loyalty.")*
> *(Rose looks at him — the correct officer, the genuine smile, the hand in hers — and understands that the afternoon has given her something she did not expect: not a suitor's answer, but an officer's. He will stand beside her — not because she asked, not because the inquiry permits, but because he is* deciding *to, the verb in the present tense, the loyalty as an action rather than a state. "Then decide," she says quietly. "And I shall refrain — from deciding for you, from hurrying you, from* requiring *anything." A pause. "It is the only loyalty I have to offer, Henry. The refraining.")*
> *("The refraining," Henry says. "I shall take it." He rises, takes up his hat — the flowers remaining on the table, forgotten and somehow right. At the door he stops. "Rose — thank you. For not deciding for me." And he is gone, into the October afternoon, deciding.)*

> 1. [examine]Give the answer — the chosen one, in daylight; the delivery. → Remembered:Her delivery; the answer is given plainly.
> 2. [social]Watch him hear it — "completely, and without moving"; the reception. → Remembered:His reception; officers hear orders completely.
> 3. [examine]Hear "the courtesy of not deciding for me" — the naming. → Remembered:The naming; the courtesy has a name.
> 4. [examine]Take "you are the only person who has refrained." → Remembered:The distinction; refraining is rare and precious.
> 5. [social]Receive the hand — "the suitor's gesture, at last"; the return. → Remembered:The hand; the council yields to the courtship.
> 6. [examine]Hear "I am deciding — the deciding is the loyalty." → Remembered:The verb; loyalty as action, present tense.
> 7. [stance]Offer the refraining — "the only loyalty I have"; the gift. → Remembered:Her gift; refraining is her loyalty's form.
> 8. [social]Watch him go — "deciding," into the afternoon; the departure. → Remembered:The departure; he leaves deciding.

### L3.S29 · [T] · Rose (alone)
*Purpose: the four secrets — Julian's, Henry's, Laurent's, Octavia's; the suitors' withheld matters, gathered.*
> *(She writes in the campaign book, late, by lamplight — the page headed "THE FOUR," in the small tight hand. Four men. Four withheld matters. She writes them out, not because she does not know them, but because writing is how she thinks: Julian's withheld matter — the thing he has not told her, the thing she has felt at the edges of his letters like a draft under a door. Henry's — the inquiry, the whisper, the unsafe; told today, in the afternoon, with flowers. Laurent's — the Frenchman's reserve, the thing behind the charm, glimpsed and not grasped. Octavia's — no, not Octavia's;* hers *about Octavia — the thing she has not admitted even to Augusta, the small cold fact she keeps turning over like a coin.)*
> *(Four men, four silences. She sits back and looks at the page, and understands that trust is not a single thing but a ledger — debits and credits, entries made and withheld, the balance shifting with every call. Julian trusts her with everything except the one thing. Henry trusted her today with the inquiry — the whole of it, the unsafe, the price. Laurent trusts her with charm and not much else. And she — she trusts none of them with the midnight ride, with the scarred knuckle's humanity, with the firewall around Creech's name. The ledger does not balance. It never balances. That is the nature of ledgers, and of men.)*
> *(She closes the book on "THE FOUR" — gently, the way one closes a book one intends to reopen. Trust strains, by increments — not broken, but* strained*, the way a rope strains before it holds or snaps. She thinks of Henry's hand in hers, the genuine smile, the deciding. She thinks of Julian's letters, the draft under the door. She thinks of the midnight streets, the mare's hoofbeats, the things she has not told. The rope holds. For now, the rope holds. She blows out the lamp.)*
> *(She turns the page, and there, in last week's hand, is the entry from the morning after the first* Lantern*: "Let them print." The ink is a little blotted; she remembers the hand shaking. She looks at the blot for a moment, then at tonight's steady script, and understands that the book has been keeping her accounts longer than she knew. Not only the war's, but her own. The shaking hand and the steady one, both hers. The ledger balances, in the end. It always did.)*

> 1. [micro]Write "THE FOUR" — the page headed; the accounting. → Remembered:Her accounting; the suitors are ledgered.
> 2. [examine]Name Julian's — "a draft under a door"; the withheld. → Remembered:Julian's silence; it's felt, not known.
> 3. [examine]Name Henry's — the inquiry, told today; the trusted. → Remembered:Henry's trust; the afternoon's gift.
> 4. [examine]Name Laurent's — the reserve behind the charm; the glimpsed. → Remembered:Laurent's reserve; charm is not confession.
> 5. [examine]Name hers — "the thing she has not admitted"; the kept. → Remembered:Her own withholding; the ledger's other side.
> 6. [examine]Take "trust is a ledger" — debits, credits, the balance. → Remembered:The metaphor; trust is accounted, not assumed.
> 7. [stance]Feel the strain — "the way a rope strains"; the tension. → Remembered:The strain; it's incremental, not broken.
> 8. [micro]Close the book — "gently"; blow out the lamp. → Remembered:The closing; the accounting rests.

### L3.S30 · [D] · Rose, Augusta
*Purpose: Augusta's verdict — on Henry, on loyalty; the general's view of the heart.*
> *(Augusta hears about Henry's call at tea — the inquiry, the whisper, the unsafe, the flowers — and her face does what it does when a dispatch contains intelligence about the heart: it becomes very still, and very kind, which is rarer than the stillness. "Horse Guards," she says at last. "Always inquiring. In my day they inquired about* everyone *— it was how they passed the time between wars." A pause. "The boy is* deciding*, child. Do you understand what that means? Most men never decide. They* drift *— into marriages, into regiments, into opinions. Deciding is the rarest thing a man does.")*
> *("He thanked me," Rose says. "For not deciding for him.")*
> *("Naturally." Augusta pours the tea — the general's hands, steady as ever. "You gave him the one thing no one else in his life gives him: the* courtesy *of his own judgment. Horse Guards decides for him. The whisper decides for him. You* refrained*." She looks at Rose over the teacup. "Refraining is the hardest thing, child. Harder than commanding. Anyone can command. Almost no one can refrain.")*
> *(Rose looks at her grandmother — the steady hands, the kind stillness — and understands that Augusta has just described the whole of her own generalship: the restraint behind the command, the silence behind the orders, the woman who could have commanded everything and chose, instead, to refrain — except when refraining would cost the war. "And the verdict, Grandmama?" she asks. "On Henry? On — " She gestures, vaguely. "— on the* heart's *part in this?")*
> *("The verdict." Augusta sets down the cup. "He is an honorable man, in a dishonorable season. The inquiry will pass — whispers always pass; they are the cheapest thing in London, cheaper than the* Lantern *itself. And you — " She looks at Rose directly. "— you will have the measure of him, when the deciding is done. Not before. The measure of a man is taken* after *he decides, child. Never before." A pause. "And the heart — the heart is not the war's enemy, Rose. It is the war's* reason*. Never forget which one you are fighting for.")*
